Their Theorem 3.4 established that the validity of the CLT depended only on the sample size tending to infinity and a Feller condition on the univariate risk space, making their result effectively “dimension-agnostic”.
During this period, statistical literature primarily focused on squared error loss in nonparametric regression, with exceptions such as
Hoffmann and Nickl (2011)
;
Carpentier (2013)
. Meanwhile, operations research literature examined inference for constrained optimization solutions with general loss functions. Inspired by the series of works by
Pflug (1991)
;
Pflug (1995)
;
Pflug (2003)
,
Vogel (2008b)
investigated risk inversion for confidence sets, but without incorporating sample-splitting. Related works include
Vogel (2008a)
;
Vogel and Seeger (2017)
and
Guigues et al. (2017)
.

•

Kim and Ramdas (2024)
later introduced the term “dimension-agnostic” to describe properties similar to those established by
Robins and van der Vaart (2006, Theorem 3.4)
, though they did not cite the earlier work. For instance, Theorem 4.2 of
Kim and Ramdas (2024)
should be compared to Theorem 3.4 of
Robins and van der Vaart (2006)
. They applied sample-splitting and CLT-based inversion to high-dimensional hypothesis testing problems, such as goodness-of-fit testing using Gaussian maximum mean discrepancy (MMD).
Chakravarti et al. (2019)
uses the similar methodology based on sample-splitting and CLT for testing the relative fit of Gaussian mixtures.
Park et al. (2025)
also employ sample-splitting and CLT for the inference on population maximum likelihood estimation under model misspecification, among other techniques. Neither
Chakravarti et al. (2019)
;
Kim and Ramdas (2024)
nor
Park et al. (2025)
developed a general theory for M-estimation such as width/diameter of the resulting confidence sets.

•

Although the explicit application of sample-splitting and CLT inversion to general M-estimation has not been previously explored, the methodological approach is a natural consequence of prior work, including
Beran and Dümbgen (1998)
,
Robins and van der Vaart (2006)
, and
Vogel (2008a)
. Consequently, we do not claim innovation in methodological front, as such confidence sets would likely have emerged given the historical trajectory of the field. Instead, our contribution lies in analyzing the properties of these confidence sets, including their validity and width.

•

M-estimators are known to exhibit locally adaptive rates of convergence, depending on problem-specific geometric factors such as curvatures
(
Kim and Pollard, 1990
;
van der Vaart and Wellner, 1996
)
. This notion of adaptivity has not been investigated within the “adaptive” inference literature on nonparametric submodels, such as
Robins and van der Vaart (2006)
and
Patschkowski and Rohde (2019)
. The concept of locally adaptive confidence sets is particularly relevant to the M-estimation framework, holding both methodological and theoretical significance. While some adaptive confidence sets have been studied in shape-restricted regression
(
Yang and Barber, 2019
;
Dümbgen, 2003
;
Bellec, 2021
)
, these approaches typically assume strong distributional conditions such as (sub-)Gaussian errors. One of the key contributions of this work is the establishment of adaptive confidence sets for general M-estimation under weaker distributional assumptions.

Research on adaptive confidence sets for nonparametric models was particularly active from the 1990s to the 2010s. These studies generally relied on concentration inequalities to establish validity, requiring precise error quantification for adaptive nonparametric estimators. Additional historical developments can be found in Chapter 8.4 of
Giné and Nickl (2021)
.

S.2

Proofs from
Section

2

S.2.1

Proof of Theorem
1

Let
\widetilde{D}_{1}
be a random element, defined on a (possibly extended) probability space, such that

\mathcal{L}(\widetilde{D}_{1})=\mathcal{L}(D_{1})
and

\widetilde{D}_{1}
is independent of
D_{2}
. Define
\widehat{\theta}_{1}:=\widehat{\theta}(D_{1})
and

\widetilde{\theta}_{1}:=\widehat{\theta}(\widetilde{D}_{1})
,
where
\widehat{\theta}(\cdot)
is a measurable function. By Berbee’s coupling lemma
(
Berbee, 1979
)
, also in Chapter 5, Lemma 5.1 of
Rio and others (2017)
, there exists a coupling satisfying

\mathbb{P}(\widetilde{D}_{1}\neq D_{1})\leq\beta(r).

Since
\{\widehat{\theta}_{1}\not=\widetilde{\theta}_{1}\}\subseteq\{\widetilde{D}_{1}\neq D_{1}\}
, it implies
\mathbb{P}(\widehat{\theta}_{1}\not=\widetilde{\theta}_{1})\leq\beta(r)
. Denote
\mathcal{L}(\widetilde{D}_{1})=\widetilde{P}^{1}
,
\mathcal{L}(D_{1})=P^{1}
, and
\mathcal{L}(D_{2})=P^{2}
. Then the miscoverage probability can be written as

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{P^{N}}(\theta(P^{N})\not\in\widehat{\mathrm{CI}}^{\dagger}_{N})

\displaystyle\quad=\mathbb{P}_{P^{N}}\left(\widehat{\mathbb{M}}_{2}(\theta(P^{N}))-\widehat{\mathbb{M}}_{2}(\widehat{\theta}_{1})\geq 0\right)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\mathbb{P}_{P^{N}}\left(\widehat{\mathbb{M}}_{2}(\theta(P^{N}))-\widehat{\mathbb{M}}_{2}(\widehat{\theta}_{1})\geq 0\,\cap\,\{\widehat{\theta}_{1}=\widetilde{\theta}_{1}\}\right)+\mathbb{P}(\widehat{\theta}_{1}\not=\widetilde{\theta}_{1})

\displaystyle\quad=\mathbb{E}_{\widetilde{P}^{1}}\left[\mathbb{P}_{P^{2}|\widetilde{P}^{1}}\left(\widehat{\mathbb{M}}_{2}(\theta(P^{N}))-\widehat{\mathbb{M}}_{2}(\widehat{\theta}_{1})\geq 0\,\cap\,\{\widehat{\theta}_{1}=\widetilde{\theta}_{1}\}\,|\,\widetilde{\theta}_{1}\right)\right]+\mathbb{P}(\widehat{\theta}_{1}\not=\widetilde{\theta}_{1}).

Conditional on
\widetilde{\theta}_{1}
,
\widehat{\mathbb{M}}_{2}(\cdot)
depends only on
D_{2}
, which is independent of
\widetilde{D}_{1}
. Applying Chebyshev’s inequality (see Equation (3) of
Pinelis (2010)
) gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{P^{2}}\left(\widehat{\mathbb{M}}_{2}(\theta(P^{N}))-\widehat{\mathbb{M}}_{2}(\widehat{\theta}_{1})\geq 0\,\cap\,\{\widehat{\theta}_{1}=\widetilde{\theta}_{1}\}\mid\widetilde{\theta}_{1}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\min\left\{\frac{\mathbb{E}_{P^{2}}[|(\widehat{\mathbb{M}}_{2}-\mathbb{M}_{2})(\theta(P^{N}))-(\widehat{\mathbb{M}}_{2}-\mathbb{M}_{2})(\widehat{\theta}_{1})|^{2}\mid\widetilde{\theta}_{1}]}{(\mathbb{M}_{2}(\widehat{\theta}_{1})-\mathbb{M}_{2}(\theta(P^{N})))^{2}},1\right\}

\displaystyle\quad=\min\left\{\frac{\mathbb{V}_{2}(\widehat{\theta}_{1})}{\mathbb{C}_{2}^{2}(\widehat{\theta}_{1})},1\right\}=\min\left\{\frac{1}{\ratio{}^{2}},1\right\}.

We conclude the result by taking the expectation over
\widetilde{D}_{1}
, which has the same law as
D_{1}
.

S.2.2

Proof of Theorem
2

The coupling argument is identical to that of the proof of
Theorem

1
. Since
\widehat{\mathbb{M}}_{2}(\theta(P^{N}))-\widehat{\mathbb{M}}_{2}(\widehat{\theta}_{1})
is unbiased, we can now invoke Cantelli’s inequality (see Equation (2) of
Pinelis (2010)
) instead of Chebyshev’s inequality. This gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{P^{2}}\left(\widehat{\mathbb{M}}_{2}(\theta(P^{N}))-\widehat{\mathbb{M}}_{2}(\widehat{\theta}_{1})\geq 0\,\cap\,\{\widehat{\theta}_{1}=\widetilde{\theta}_{1}\}\,|\,\widetilde{\theta}_{1}\right)

\displaystyle\quad=\mathbb{P}_{P^{2}}\left(\frac{\widehat{\mathbb{M}}_{2}(\theta(P^{N}))-\widehat{\mathbb{M}}_{2}(\widehat{\theta}_{1})+\widehat{\mathbb{C}}_{2}}{\widehat{\mathbb{V}}^{1/2}_{2}}\geq\frac{\widehat{\mathbb{C}}_{2}}{\widehat{\mathbb{V}}^{1/2}_{2}}\,\cap\,\{\widehat{\theta}_{1}=\widetilde{\theta}_{1}\}\,|\,\widetilde{\theta}_{1}\right)\leq\frac{1}{1+\widehat{\mathbb{C}}_{2}^{2}/\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_{2}}=\frac{1}{1+\ratio^{2}}.

We conclude the result by taking the expectation over
\widetilde{D}_{1}
, which has the same law as
D_{1}
.

S.3

Proofs from
Section

3

S.3.1

Proof of
Theorem

3

Since
P^{1}
and
P^{2}
are independent, we can write the miscoverage probability as

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{P^{N}}\left(\widehat{\mathbb{M}}_{2}(\theta(P^{N}))-\widehat{\mathbb{M}}_{2}(\widehat{\theta}_{1})\geq 0\right)=\E_{P^{1}}\left[\mathbb{P}_{P^{2}|P^{1}}\left(\widehat{\mathbb{M}}_{2}(\theta(P^{N}))-\widehat{\mathbb{M}}_{2}(\widehat{\theta}_{1})\geq 0\right)\right].

Conditionally on
D_{1}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{P^{2}|P^{1}}\left(\widehat{\mathbb{M}}_{2}(\theta(P^{N}))-\widehat{\mathbb{M}}_{2}(\widehat{\theta}_{1})\geq 0\right)

\displaystyle\quad=\mathbb{P}_{P^{2}|P^{1}}\left(\sum_{i\in I_{2}}m_{\theta(P^{N})}(Z_{i})-m_{\widehat{\theta}}(Z_{i})\geq 0\right)

\displaystyle\quad=\mathbb{P}_{P^{2}|P^{1}}\left(-\sum_{i\in I_{2}}\widehat{\xi}_{i}/(n_{2}\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_{2}^{1/2})\geq\widehat{\mathbb{C}}_{2}/\widehat{\mathbb{V}}^{1/2}_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\mathbb{P}\left(Z\geq\widehat{\mathbb{C}}_{2}/\widehat{\mathbb{V}}^{1/2}_{2}\,\bigg|\,D_{1}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\left|\mathbb{P}_{P^{2}|P^{1}}\left(-\sum_{i\in I_{2}}\widehat{\xi}_{i}/(n_{2}\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_{2}^{1/2})\geq\widehat{\mathbb{C}}_{2}/\widehat{\mathbb{V}}^{1/2}_{2}\right)-\mathbb{P}\left(Z\geq\widehat{\mathbb{C}}_{2}/\widehat{\mathbb{V}}^{1/2}_{2}\,\bigg|\,D_{1}\right)\right|

where
Z
denotes a standard Normal random variable. Conditioning on
D_{1}
, the standardized sum
-n_{2}^{-1}\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_{2}^{-1/2}\sum_{i\in I_{2}}\widehat{\xi}_{i}
is a sum of independent mean-zero random variables with unit variance. Hence, the last remainder term can be controlled by a non-uniform Berry-Esseen bound (such as Theorem 2.1 of
Chen and Shao (2001)
):

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{P}_{P^{2}|P^{1}}\left(-\sum_{i\in I_{2}}\widehat{\xi}_{i}/(n_{2}\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_{2}^{1/2})\geq\widehat{\mathbb{C}}_{2}/\widehat{\mathbb{V}}^{1/2}_{2}\right)-\mathbb{P}\left(Z\geq\widehat{\mathbb{C}}_{2}/\widehat{\mathbb{V}}^{1/2}_{2}\,\bigg|\,D_{1}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\quad\leq C\sum_{i\in I_{2}}\mathbb{E}_{P_{i}}\left[\frac{|\widehat{\xi}_{i}|^{2}}{(n_{2}^{2}\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_{2})(1+\widehat{\mathbb{C}}_{2}/\widehat{\mathbb{V}}^{1/2}_{2})^{2}}\min\left\{1,\,\frac{|\widehat{\xi}_{i}|}{n_{2}\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_{2}^{1/2}(1+\widehat{\mathbb{C}}_{2}/\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_{2}^{1/2})}\right\}\bigg|D_{1}\right],

where
C
is a universal constant. Finally, taking expectation over
D_{1}
and using linearity of expectation concludes the proof.

S.3.2

Proof of
Theorem

4

We first establish the following lemma, of which
Theorem

4
is a direct consequence.

Lemma 28
.

Let
\mathcal{H}_{0}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{H}_{N}
be a filtration, supporting
\widehat{\xi}_{1},\ldots,\widehat{\xi}_{N}
defined in (
13
), with
\widehat{\theta}_{1}
assumed
\mathcal{H}_{0}
-measurable. Define the martingale approximation

\widetilde{\xi}_{i}=\sum_{r=1}^{N}(\E[\widehat{\xi}_{r}|\mathcal{H}_{i}]-\E[\widehat{\xi}_{r}|\mathcal{H}_{i-1}])\quad\textrm{and}\quad U=\sum_{i=1}^{N}(\widehat{\xi}_{i}-\E[\widehat{\xi}_{i}|\mathcal{H}_{N}]+\E[\widehat{\xi}_{i}|\mathcal{H}_{0}]).

(E.1)

Set
\widetilde{\mathbb{V}}=\mathrm{Var}(\sum_{i=1}^{N}\widetilde{\xi}_{i}|\mathcal{H}_{0})
and
\widehat{\mathbb{C}}=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\E[m_{\widehat{\theta}_{1}}(Z_{i})-m_{\theta(P^{N})}(Z_{i})|\mathcal{H}_{0}]
. For
\delta\in(0,\infty)
, define

\begin{split}L_{2\delta}:=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\E\left[\left|\frac{\widetilde{\xi}_{i}}{\widetilde{\mathbb{V}}^{1/2}}\right|^{2+2\delta}\,|\,\mathcal{H}_{0}\right]\quad\textrm{and}\quad M_{2\delta}:=\E\left(\left|\sum_{i=1}^{N}\E\left[\frac{\widetilde{\xi}_{i}^{2}}{\widetilde{\mathbb{V}}}\mid\mathcal{H}_{i-1}\right]-1\right|^{1+\delta}\mid\mathcal{H}_{0}\right)\quad.\end{split}

(E.2)

Then for any
\eta>0
, it holds that

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}_{P^{N}}\!\left(\theta(P^{N})\notin\widehat{\mathrm{CI}}_{N}^{\dagger}\right)&\leq\mathbb{E}\left[1-\Phi\left((1-\eta)\widehat{\mathbb{C}}/\widetilde{\mathbb{V}}^{1/2}\right)\right]\\
&\quad+\mathbb{E}\left[\min\left\{1,C_{\delta}\frac{(L_{2\delta}+M_{2\delta})^{1/(3+2\delta)}}{1+|(1-\eta)\widehat{\mathbb{C}}/\widetilde{\mathbb{V}}^{1/2}|^{2+2\delta}}\right\}\right]\\
&\quad+\mathbb{P}\left(U<-\eta\,\widehat{\mathbb{C}}\right),\end{split}

(E.3)

where
C_{\delta}
is a constant depending only on
\delta
.

Proof of
Lemma

28
.

By a telescoping identity,
\sum_{i=1}^{N}\widehat{\xi}_{i}=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\widetilde{\xi}_{i}+U
. Since
\widehat{\theta}_{1}
is
\mathcal{H}_{0}
-measurable, conditioning on
\mathcal{H}_{0}
gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\theta(P^{N})\notin\widehat{\mathrm{CI}}_{N}^{\dagger}\mid\mathcal{H}_{0}\right)

\displaystyle\quad=\mathbb{P}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}m_{\theta(P^{N})}(Z_{i})-m_{\widehat{\theta}_{1}}(Z_{i})\geq 0\mid\mathcal{H}_{0}\right)

\displaystyle\quad=\mathbb{P}\left(-\sum_{i=1}^{N}\widehat{\xi}_{i}\geq\widehat{\mathbb{C}}\mid\mathcal{H}_{0}\right)

\displaystyle\quad=\mathbb{P}\left(-\sum_{i=1}^{N}\widetilde{\xi}_{i}/\widetilde{\mathbb{V}}^{1/2}\geq(\widehat{\mathbb{C}}+U)/\widetilde{\mathbb{V}}^{1/2}\mid\mathcal{H}_{0}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\mathbb{P}\left(-\sum_{i=1}^{N}\widetilde{\xi}_{i}/\widetilde{\mathbb{V}}^{1/2}\geq(1-\eta)\widehat{\mathbb{C}}/\widetilde{\mathbb{V}}^{1/2}\mid\mathcal{H}_{0}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(U<-\eta\widehat{\mathbb{C}}\mid\mathcal{H}_{0}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\mathbb{P}\left(Z\geq(1-\eta)\widehat{\mathbb{C}}/\widetilde{\mathbb{V}}^{1/2}\mid\mathcal{H}_{0}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(U<-\eta\widehat{\mathbb{C}}\mid\mathcal{H}_{0}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\left|\mathbb{P}\left(-\sum_{i=1}^{N}\widetilde{\xi}_{i}/\widetilde{\mathbb{V}}^{1/2}\geq(1-\eta)\widehat{\mathbb{C}}/\widetilde{\mathbb{V}}^{1/2}\mid\mathcal{H}_{0}\right)-\mathbb{P}\left(Z\geq(1-\eta)\widehat{\mathbb{C}}/\widetilde{\mathbb{V}}^{1/2}\mid\mathcal{H}_{0}\right)\right|,

where
Z
follows a standard normal distribution and is independent of
\mathcal{H}_{N}
. On the
\mathcal{H}_{0}
-measurable event
\{L_{2\delta}+M_{2\delta}\leq 1\}
, Theorem 1 of
Haeusler and Joos (1988)
gives, and obtain

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{P}\left(-\sum_{i=1}^{N}\widetilde{\xi}_{i}/\widetilde{\mathbb{V}}^{1/2}\geq(1-\eta)\widehat{\mathbb{C}}/\widetilde{\mathbb{V}}^{1/2}\mid\mathcal{H}_{0}\right)-\mathbb{P}\left(Z\geq(1-\eta)\widehat{\mathbb{C}}/\widetilde{\mathbb{V}}^{1/2}\mid\mathcal{H}_{0}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\quad\leq C_{\delta}\frac{(L_{2\delta}+M_{2\delta})^{1/(3+2\delta)}}{1+|(1-\eta)\widehat{\mathbb{C}}/\widetilde{\mathbb{V}}^{1/2}|^{2+2\delta}}+\mathbf{1}\{L_{2\delta}+M_{2\delta}>1\},

where
C_{\delta}
is a constant only depending on
\delta
. In the proof of Theorem 1 of
Haeusler and Joos (1988)
, it is stated that the following bound

\displaystyle\frac{L_{2\delta}+M_{2\delta}}{1+|(1-\eta)\widehat{\mathbb{C}}/\widetilde{\mathbb{V}}^{1/2}|^{2+2\delta}}

is valid for
\mathbf{1}\{L_{2\delta}+M_{2\delta}>1\}
, which can further bounded by the trivial bound of 1. Taking expectations over
\mathcal{H}_{0}
and combining the terms concludes the proof.
∎

Proof of
Theorem

4
.

For
i\in I_{2}
, the definition of
\widehat{\xi}_{i}
and
\mathcal{H}_{k}
-measurability of
\widehat{\theta}_{1}
together imply that
\E[\widehat{\xi}_{i}|\mathcal{H}_{n_{2}}]=\widehat{\xi}_{i}
and
\E[\widehat{\xi}_{i}|\mathcal{H}_{0}]=0
. Then we have

\displaystyle U=\sum_{i\in I_{2}}(\widehat{\xi}_{i}-\E[\widehat{\xi}_{i}|\mathcal{H}_{n_{2}}]+\E[\widehat{\xi}_{i}|\mathcal{H}_{0}])=0\quad\text{and}\quad\sum_{i\in I_{2}}\widehat{\xi}_{i}=\sum_{i\in I_{2}}\widetilde{\xi}_{i}.

Moreover,

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbb{C}}=\sum_{i\in I_{2}}\E[m_{\widehat{\theta}_{1}}(Z_{i})-m_{\theta(P^{N})}(Z_{i})|\mathcal{H}_{0}]=n_{2}\widehat{\mathbb{C}}_{2},

and

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbb{V}}=\mathrm{Var}\left(\sum_{i\in I_{2}}\widetilde{\xi}_{i}|\mathcal{H}_{0}\right)=n_{2}^{2}\mathrm{Var}\left(\frac{1}{n_{2}}\sum_{i\in I_{2}}\widehat{\xi}_{i}|\mathcal{H}_{0}\right)=n_{2}^{2}\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_{2}.

The factors of
n_{2}
cancels, yielding
\ratio=\widehat{\mathbb{C}}_{2}/\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_{2}^{1/2}=\widehat{\mathbb{C}}/\widetilde{\mathbb{V}}^{1/2}
. Since
\widehat{\mathbb{C}}_{2}\geq 0
almost surely by definition, it follows that
\mathbb{P}(U<-\eta\widehat{\mathbb{C}}_{2}\mid\mathcal{H}_{0})=0
for any
\eta>0
. The result is obtained from applying
Lemma

28
and taking
\eta\to 0
.
∎

S.4

Proofs from
Section

4

S.4.1

Proofs of
Theorem

5
and
Theorem

6

Proof of
Theorem

5
.

Denote
\overline{D}_{\ell}:=\{Z_{i}\,:\,i\in S_{\ell}\}
for
1\leq\ell\leq B
. By iterative application of Berbee’s coupling lemma
(
Berbee, 1979
)
, construct independent copies
\widetilde{D}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{D}_{B}
such that
\mathcal{L}(\widetilde{D}_{\ell})=\mathcal{L}(\overline{D}_{\ell})
with
\mathbb{P}(\overline{D}_{\ell}\neq\widetilde{D}_{\ell})\leq\beta^{\dagger}(r)
at each coupling step. Define test functions

\displaystyle\widehat{\phi}_{\ell}:=\mathbf{1}\{\theta(P^{N})\notin\widehat{\mathrm{CI}}^{\dagger}_{\ell}\}\quad\text{and}\quad\widetilde{\phi}_{\ell}:=\mathbf{1}\{\theta(P^{N})\notin\widetilde{\mathrm{CI}}^{\dagger}_{\ell}\},

where
\widehat{\mathrm{CI}}^{\dagger}_{\ell}
and
\widetilde{\mathrm{CI}}^{\dagger}_{\ell}
are the confidence sets constructed from
\overline{D}_{\ell}
and
\widetilde{D}_{\ell}
respectively. By the union bound at each
B-1
coupling steps,
\mathbb{P}(\exists\,\ell\in\{1,\ldots,B\}:\widehat{\phi}_{\ell}\neq\widetilde{\phi}_{\ell})\leq(B-1)\beta^{\dagger}(r).
On the complimentary event
\{\widehat{\phi}_{\ell}=\widetilde{\phi}_{\ell}\textrm{ for all }\ell\}
, the confidence sets
\widetilde{\mathrm{CI}}^{\dagger}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{\mathrm{CI}}^{\dagger}_{B}
are independent. Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{P^{N}}\!\left(\theta(P^{N})\notin\widehat{\mathrm{CI}}^{\texttt{DS}}_{N,\alpha}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{P^{N}}\!\left(\bigcap_{\ell=1}^{B}\left\{\theta(P^{N})\notin\widehat{\mathrm{CI}}^{\dagger}_{\ell}\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}_{P^{N}}\!\left(\bigcap_{\ell=1}^{B}\left\{\theta(P^{N})\notin\widetilde{\mathrm{CI}}^{\dagger}_{\ell}\right\}\right)+(B-1)\beta^{\dagger}(r)

\displaystyle=\prod_{\ell=1}^{B}\mathbb{P}_{P^{N}}\!\left(\theta(P^{N})\notin\widetilde{\mathrm{CI}}^{\dagger}_{\ell}\right)+(B-1)\beta^{\dagger}(r)

\displaystyle\leq\left(p+\mathfrak{R}_{N_{0},P^{N}}\right)^{\!B}+(B-1)\beta^{\dagger}(r)

\displaystyle\leq\alpha\left(1+p^{-1}\mathfrak{R}_{N_{0},P^{N}}\right)^{B}+(B-1)\beta^{\dagger}(r),

where
N_{0}=\min_{\ell}|S_{\ell}|
denotes the smallest bin size, the third line uses independence, the fourth line applies (
21
) and the last line uses
p^{B}\leq\alpha
.
∎

Proof of
Theorem

6
.

By Berbee’s lemma, construct
\widetilde{D}_{1}
with
\mathcal{L}(D_{1})=\mathcal{L}(\widetilde{D}_{1})
, and with
\widetilde{D}_{1}
and
D_{2}
independent. Let
\widetilde{\theta}_{1}:=\widehat{\theta}(\widetilde{D}_{1})
and define

\Omega:=\left\{\mathbb{M}_{2}(\theta(P^{N}))-\mathbb{M}_{2}(\widetilde{\theta}_{1})\geq\widehat{\mathbb{M}}_{2}(\theta(P^{N}))-\widehat{\mathbb{M}}_{2}(\widetilde{\theta}_{1})-\widehat{t}_{\alpha}(\theta(P^{N}),\widetilde{\theta}_{1})\right\}.

By (
23
), we have
\mathbb{P}(\Omega^{c})\leq\alpha
.
Conditioning on
\Omega\,\cap\,\{D_{1}=\widetilde{D}_{1}\}
, the basic inequality (
4
) gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{P^{N}}\left(\theta(P^{N})\notin\widehat{\mathrm{CI}}^{\texttt{LCB}}_{N,\alpha}\,\cap\,\Omega\,\cap\,\{D_{1}=\widetilde{D}_{1}\}\right)

\displaystyle\quad=\mathbb{P}_{P^{N}}\left(\widehat{\mathbb{M}}_{2}(\theta(P^{N}))-\widehat{\mathbb{M}}_{2}(\widetilde{\theta}_{1})-\widehat{t}_{\alpha}(\theta(P^{N}),\widetilde{\theta}_{1})\geq 0\,\cap\,\Omega\,\cap\,\{D_{1}=\widetilde{D}_{1}\}\right)

\displaystyle\quad=\mathbb{P}_{P^{N}}\left(\mathbb{M}_{2}(\theta(P^{N}))-\mathbb{M}_{2}(\widetilde{\theta}_{1})\geq 0\,\cap\,\Omega\,\cap\,\{D_{1}=\widetilde{D}_{1}\}\right)=0.

Putting together, we conclude

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{P^{N}}\!\left(\theta(P^{N})\notin\widehat{\mathrm{CI}}^{\texttt{LCB}}_{N,\alpha}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}(\Omega^{c})+\mathbb{P}(D_{1}\neq\widetilde{D}_{1})\leq\alpha+\beta(r).

∎

S.4.2

Proofs of
Theorem

7
—
Theorem

11

Proof of
Theorem

7
.

The goal is to prove (
25
) with
\beta(r)=0
holding from independence. The result then follows by
Theorem

6
. Observe

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{P^{2}}\left(\frac{1}{n_{2}}\sum_{i\in I_{2}}\widehat{\xi}_{i}\geq\widehat{t}_{\alpha}(\theta(P^{N}),\widehat{\theta}_{1})|D_{1}\right)

\displaystyle\quad=\mathbb{P}_{P^{2}}\left(\frac{1}{n_{2}}\sum_{i\in I_{2}}\widehat{\xi}_{i}\geq n_{2}^{-1/2}z_{\alpha}\widehat{\sigma}_{\theta,\widehat{\theta}_{1}}|D_{1}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\left|\mathbb{P}_{P^{2}}\left(n_{2}^{-1/2}\widehat{\sigma}_{\theta,\widehat{\theta}_{1}}^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_{2}}\widehat{\xi}_{i}\geq z_{\alpha}|D_{1}\right)-\mathbb{P}(Z\geq z_{\alpha})\right|+\mathbb{P}(Z\geq z_{\alpha})

\displaystyle\quad\leq C\left\{\sum_{i\in I_{2}}\mathbb{P}_{P_{i}}(\widehat{\xi}^{2}_{i}>V^{2}|D_{1})+M^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_{2}}|\mathbb{E}_{P_{i}}[\overline{\xi}_{i}|D_{1}]|+M^{-3}\sum_{i\in I_{2}}\mathbb{E}_{P_{i}}[|\overline{\xi}_{i}|^{3}|D_{1}]\right\}+\alpha

where the last inequality is by Corollary 1.1 of
Bentkus et al. (1996)
. The result is obtained after taking expectation over
D_{1}
.
∎

Proof of
Theorem

8
.

Theorem

7
holds after truncating at 1, such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{P^{N}}\left(\theta(P^{N})\not\in\widehat{\mathrm{CI}}^{\mathtt{CLT}}_{N,\alpha}\right)\leq\alpha

\displaystyle\quad+\E_{P^{1}}\left[\min\left\{1,C\left(\sum_{i\in I_{2}}\mathbb{P}_{P_{i}}(\widehat{\xi}^{2}_{i}>V^{2}|D_{1})+\frac{\sum_{i\in I_{2}}|\mathbb{E}_{P_{i}}[\overline{\xi}_{i}|D_{1}]|}{M}+\frac{\sum_{i\in I_{2}}\mathbb{E}_{P_{i}}[|\overline{\xi}_{i}|^{3}|D_{1}]}{M^{3}}\right)\right\}\right].

Since this holds for any
V
, we can further bound the remainder term with the choice
V=n_{2}\widehat{\mathbb{V}}^{1/2}_{2}
. The following proof is based on
Katz (1963)
.
Define
\mathcal{G}
to be the class of all non-decreasing functions
g:(0,\infty)\mapsto(0,\infty)
and that
x/g(x)
is non-decreasing on
(0,\infty)
. Fix
g\in\mathcal{G}
. Using the properties of
\mathcal{G}
such that
g\in\mathcal{G}
is non-decreasing and non-negative, we have

\displaystyle\{|\widehat{\xi}_{i}|\geq n_{2}\widehat{\mathbb{V}}^{1/2}_{2}\}\subseteq\{|\widehat{\xi}_{i}|g(|\widehat{\xi}_{i}|)\geq n_{2}\widehat{\mathbb{V}}^{1/2}_{2}g(\widehat{\mathbb{V}}^{1/2}_{2})\}.

(E.4)

Then we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in I_{2}}\mathbb{P}_{P_{i}}(\widehat{\xi}^{2}_{i}>n_{2}^{2}\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_{2}|D_{1})\leq\sum_{i\in I_{2}}\mathbb{P}_{P_{i}}(\widehat{\xi}^{2}_{i}g(|\widehat{\xi_{i}}|)>n_{2}^{2}\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_{2}g(n_{2}\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_{2}^{1/2})|D_{1})\leq\frac{\sum_{i\in I_{2}}\E_{P_{i}}[\widehat{\xi}^{2}_{i}g(|\widehat{\xi_{i}}|)|D_{1}]}{n_{2}^{2}\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_{2}g(n_{2}\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_{2}^{1/2})},

where
\mathbf{1}\{A>B\}\leq A/B
for
A,B\geq 0
. Next, observe that

\displaystyle n_{2}^{-1}\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_{2}^{-1/2}\widehat{\xi}_{i}

\displaystyle=n_{2}^{-1}\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_{2}^{-1/2}\widehat{\xi}_{i}\mathbf{1}\{|\widehat{\xi}_{i}|\leq n_{2}\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_{2}^{1/2}\}+n_{2}^{-1}\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_{2}^{-1/2}\widehat{\xi}_{i}\mathbf{1}\{|\widehat{\xi}_{i}|>n_{2}\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_{2}^{1/2}\}

\displaystyle=\overline{\xi}_{i}+n_{2}^{-1}\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_{2}^{-1/2}\widehat{\xi}_{i}\mathbf{1}\{|\widehat{\xi}_{i}|>n_{2}\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_{2}^{1/2}\}.

Since
\E_{P_{i}}[\widehat{\xi}_{i}|D_{1}]=0
, we have

\displaystyle\E_{P_{i}}[\overline{\xi}_{i}|D_{1}]=-n_{2}^{-1}\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_{2}^{-1/2}\E_{P_{i}}[\widehat{\xi}_{i}\mathbf{1}\{|\widehat{\xi}_{i}|>n_{2}\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_{2}^{1/2}\}|D_{1}].

(E.5)

Next, we bound the variance. Observe

\displaystyle 1-M^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sum_{i\in I_{2}}\E_{P_{i}}[\widehat{\xi}_{i}^{2}|D_{1}]}{n_{2}^{2}\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_{2}}-\sum_{i\in I_{2}}\E_{P_{i}}[\overline{\xi}_{i}^{2}|D_{1}]+\sum_{i\in I_{2}}(\E_{P_{i}}[\overline{\xi}_{i}|D_{1}])^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\sum_{i\in I_{2}}\E_{P_{i}}[\widehat{\xi}_{i}^{2}\mathbf{1}\{|\widehat{\xi}_{i}|\geq n_{2}\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_{2}^{1/2}\}|D_{1}]}{n_{2}^{2}\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_{2}}\leq\frac{2\sum_{i\in I_{2}}\E_{P_{i}}[|\widehat{\xi}_{i}|^{2}g(|\widehat{\xi}_{i}|)|D_{1}]}{n_{2}^{2}\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_{2}g(n_{2}\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_{2}^{1/2})},

where we used (
E.5
). Next,

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in I_{2}}|\mathbb{E}_{P_{i}}[\overline{\xi}_{i}|D_{1}]|

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\in I_{2}}|\E_{P_{i}}[n_{2}^{-1}\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_{2}^{-1/2}\widehat{\xi}_{i}\mathbf{1}\{|\widehat{\xi}_{i}|>n_{2}\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_{2}^{1/2}\}|D_{1}]|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i\in I_{2}}\E_{P_{i}}[n_{2}^{-1}\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_{2}^{-1/2}|\widehat{\xi}_{i}|\mathbf{1}\{|\widehat{\xi}_{i}|>n_{2}\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_{2}^{1/2}\}|D_{1}]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i\in I_{2}}\E_{P_{i}}[n_{2}^{-1}\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_{2}^{-1/2}|\widehat{\xi}_{i}|\mathbf{1}\{|\widehat{\xi}_{i}|g(|\widehat{\xi}_{i}|)>n_{2}\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_{2}^{1/2}g(n_{2}\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_{2}^{1/2})\}|D_{1}]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sum_{i\in I_{2}}\E_{P_{i}}[|\widehat{\xi}_{i}|^{2}g(|\widehat{\xi}_{i}|)|D_{1}]}{n_{2}^{2}\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_{2}g(n_{2}\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_{2}^{1/2})}.

Finally, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in I_{2}}\mathbb{E}_{P_{i}}[|\overline{\xi}_{i}|^{3}|D_{1}]

\displaystyle=\frac{\sum_{i\in I_{2}}\mathbb{E}_{P_{i}}[|\widehat{\xi}_{i}|^{3}\mathbf{1}\{|\widehat{\xi}_{i}|\leq n_{2}\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_{2}^{1/2}\}|D_{1}]}{n_{2}^{3}\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_{2}^{3/2}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\in I_{2}}\mathbb{E}_{P_{i}}\left[\frac{|\widehat{\xi}_{i}|^{2}|\widehat{\xi}_{i}|g(|\widehat{\xi}_{i}|)\mathbf{1}\{|\widehat{\xi}_{i}|\leq n_{2}\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_{2}^{1/2}\}}{n_{2}^{2}\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_{2}\cdot n_{2}\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_{2}^{1/2}g(|\widehat{\xi}_{i}|)}\bigg|D_{1}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sum_{i\in I_{2}}\mathbb{E}_{P_{i}}[|\widehat{\xi}_{i}|^{2}g(|\widehat{\xi}_{i}|)|D_{1}]}{n_{2}^{2}\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_{2}g(n_{2}\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_{2}^{1/2})}.

We now assume that

\displaystyle\frac{\sum_{i\in I_{2}}\E_{P_{i}}[|\widehat{\xi}_{i}|^{2}g(|\widehat{\xi}_{i}|)|D_{1}]}{n_{2}^{2}\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_{2}g(n_{2}\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_{2}^{1/2})}\leq\frac{1}{3}.

Under this assumption, we have
1/3\leq M^{3}
, and thus
M
is bounded from below. Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{P^{N}}\left(\theta(P^{N})\not\in\widehat{\mathrm{CI}}^{\mathtt{CLT}}_{N,\alpha}\right)\leq\alpha

\displaystyle\quad+\min\left\{1,C\,\mathbb{E}_{P^{1}}\left[\sum_{i\in I_{2}}\mathbb{P}_{P_{i}}(\widehat{\xi}^{2}_{i}>\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_{2}|D_{1})+\frac{\sum_{i\in I_{2}}|\mathbb{E}_{P_{i}}[\overline{\xi}_{i}|D_{1}]|}{M}+\frac{\sum_{i\in I_{2}}\mathbb{E}_{P_{i}}[|\overline{\xi}_{i}|^{3}|D_{1}]}{M^{3}}\right]\right\}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\alpha+\min\left\{1,C\frac{\sum_{i\in I_{2}}\mathbb{E}_{P_{i}}[|\widehat{\xi}_{i}|^{2}g(|\widehat{\xi}_{i}|)|D_{1}]}{n_{2}^{2}\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_{2}g(n_{2}\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_{2}^{1/2})}\right\}.

Note that choosing
C\geq 3
, the bound is still valid under the case where

\displaystyle\frac{\sum_{i\in I_{2}}\E_{P_{i}}[|\widehat{\xi}_{i}|^{2}g(|\widehat{\xi}_{i}|)|D_{1}]}{n_{2}^{2}\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_{2}g(n_{2}\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_{2}^{1/2})}\geq\frac{1}{3},

since the result follows trivially.
Finally, choosing
g(|x|)=\min\{|x|,n_{2}\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_{2}^{1/2}\}
, we conclude

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{P^{N}}\left(\theta(P^{N})\not\in\widehat{\mathrm{CI}}^{\mathtt{CLT}}_{N,\alpha}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\alpha+\min\left\{1,C\frac{\sum_{i\in I_{2}}\mathbb{E}_{P_{i}}[|\widehat{\xi}_{i}|^{2}\min(|\widehat{\xi}_{i}|,n_{2}\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_{2}^{1/2})|D_{1}]}{n_{2}^{3}\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_{2}^{3/2}}\right\}

\displaystyle\quad=\alpha+\min\left\{1,C\sum_{i\in I_{2}}\mathbb{E}_{P_{i}}\left[\frac{|\widehat{\xi}_{i}|^{2}}{n_{2}^{2}\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_{2}}\min\left\{\frac{|\widehat{\xi}_{i}|}{n_{2}\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_{2}^{1/2}},1\right\}\bigg|D_{1}\right]\right\}.

∎

Proof of
Theorem

9
.

The proof is identical to that of
Theorem

7
, except that we use Corollary 1.2 of
Bentkus et al. (1996)
.
∎

Proof of
Theorem

10
.

First, we prove the case with
\alpha\leq 1/2
. We denote, conditionally on
D_{1}
,

\displaystyle T_{n_{2}}=n_{2}^{-1/2}\widehat{\sigma}_{\theta,\widehat{\theta}_{1}}^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_{2}}\widehat{\xi}_{i},\quad S_{n_{2}}=\sum_{i\in I_{2}}\widehat{\xi}_{i},\quad\text{and}\quad V_{n_{2}}^{2}=\sum_{i\in I_{2}}\widehat{\xi}_{i}^{2}.

By the algebraic identity (1.2) of
Giné et al. (1997)
, the events
\{T_{n_{2}}\geq z_{\alpha}\}
and
\{S_{n_{2}}/V_{n_{2}}\geq z_{\alpha,n_{2}}\}
coincide, so we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{P^{N}}\left(\theta(P^{N})\not\in\widehat{\mathrm{CI}}^{\mathtt{CLT}}_{N,\alpha}\right)=\mathbb{E}_{P^{1}}[\mathbb{P}_{P^{2}}(T_{n_{2}}\geq z_{\alpha}|D_{1})]=\mathbb{E}_{P^{1}}\left[\mathbb{P}_{P^{2}}\left(\frac{S_{n_{2}}}{V_{n_{2}}}\geq z_{\alpha,n_{2}}\bigg|D_{1}\right)\right].

The bound
R_{1}
follows by the same argument as in
Theorem

7
, using Theorem 2 of
Robinson and Wang (2005)
when
R_{*}\leq C/(1+z_{\alpha,n_{2}})^{2}
, for some universal constant
C
.

For
\alpha\geq 1/2
, it follows that
z_{\alpha}\leq 0
. Consider the following objects:

\displaystyle\widetilde{T}_{n_{2}}=n_{2}^{-1/2}\widehat{\sigma}_{\theta,\widehat{\theta}_{1}}^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_{2}}(-\widehat{\xi}_{i}),\quad\widetilde{S}_{n_{2}}=\sum_{i\in I_{2}}(-\widehat{\xi}_{i}),\quad\text{and}\quad\widetilde{V}_{n_{2}}^{2}=V_{n_{2}}^{2}=\sum_{i\in I_{2}}\widehat{\xi}_{i}^{2}.

Then

\displaystyle\{S_{n_{2}}\geq z_{\alpha,n_{2}}V_{n_{2}}\}

\displaystyle=\{S_{n_{2}}\geq-z_{1-\alpha,n_{2}}V_{n_{2}}\}=\{\widetilde{S}_{n_{2}}\leq z_{1-\alpha,n_{2}}\widetilde{V}_{n_{2}}\}.

Using equation (1.6) of
Robinson and Wang (2005)
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{P^{N}}\left(\theta(P^{N})\not\in\widehat{\mathrm{CI}}^{\mathtt{CLT}}_{N,\alpha}\right)

\displaystyle=1-\mathbb{E}_{P^{1}}\left[\mathbb{P}_{P^{2}}\left(\frac{\widetilde{S}_{n_{2}}}{\widetilde{V}_{n_{2}}}\geq z_{1-\alpha,n_{2}}\bigg|D_{1}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq 1-(1-\Phi(z_{1-\alpha,n_{2}}))\exp(-AR_{*})

\displaystyle=\Phi(z_{1-\alpha,n_{2}})\exp(-AR_{*})

when
R_{*}\leq(1+z_{1-\alpha,n_{2}})^{2}/A
and
A>0
is a universal constant. Finally, using the identity,

\displaystyle z_{1-\alpha,n_{2}}=-z_{\alpha,n_{2}}\quad\text{and}\quad\Phi(z_{1-\alpha,n_{2}})=1-\Phi(z_{\alpha,n_{2}}),

we conclude the result.

It remains to establish
R_{2}
. We use Lemma 2.2 and Theorem 2.8 of
Giné et al. (1997)
, asserts that stochastic boundedness of the self-normalized statistics implies sub-Gaussianity. Denote

\displaystyle c(2)=\sqrt{2}\left(\frac{4e}{3}+1\right)^{2}.

For any
a
, Lemma 2.2

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{P^{2}}\left[\left|\frac{S_{n_{2}}}{V_{n_{2}}}\right|^{2}\bigg|D_{1}\right]

\displaystyle\leq c(2)\left(\mathbb{E}_{P^{2}}\left[\left|\frac{S_{n_{2}}}{V_{n_{2}}}\right|\bigg|D_{1}\right]\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq c(2)\left\{a+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{P^{2}}\left[\left(\frac{S_{n_{2}}}{V_{n_{2}}}\right)^{2}\bigg|D_{1}\right]}\sqrt{\mathbb{P}_{P^{2}}\left(\left|\frac{S_{n_{2}}}{V_{n_{2}}}\right|\geq a\bigg|D_{1}\right)}\right\}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2c(2)\left\{a^{2}+\mathbb{E}_{P^{2}}\left[\left(\frac{S_{n_{2}}}{V_{n_{2}}}\right)^{2}\bigg|D_{1}\right]\mathbb{P}_{P^{2}}\left(\left|\frac{S_{n_{2}}}{V_{n_{2}}}\right|\geq a\bigg|D_{1}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\leq 2c(2)\left\{a^{2}+\mathbb{E}_{P^{2}}\left[\left(\frac{S_{n_{2}}}{V_{n_{2}}}\right)^{2}\bigg|D_{1}\right]\left(\mathbb{P}_{P^{2}}(|Z|\geq a|D_{1})+C^{\prime}R_{*}\right)\right\}

where the first inequality follows from the equation 2.8 of
Giné et al. (1997)
, the second by Hölder’s inequality, and the last follows by Corollary 1.2 of
Bentkus et al. (1996)
, which we leveraged in
Theorem

9
. Here,
C^{\prime}>0
is a universal constant. Choose
a
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{P^{2}}(|Z|\geq a|D_{1})=\frac{1}{4c(2)},

and fix
a
as a universal constant. Rearranging, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{P^{2}}\left[\left|\frac{S_{n_{2}}}{V_{n_{2}}}\right|^{2}\bigg|D_{1}\right]\leq 2c(2)a^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}_{P^{2}}\left[\left|\frac{S_{n_{2}}}{V_{n_{2}}}\right|^{2}\bigg|D_{1}\right]+2c(2)C^{\prime}R_{*}\mathbb{E}_{P^{2}}\left[\left|\frac{S_{n_{2}}}{V_{n_{2}}}\right|^{2}\bigg|D_{1}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\Leftrightarrow\left(\frac{1}{2}-2c(2)C^{\prime}R_{*}\right)_{+}\mathbb{E}_{P^{2}}\left[\left|\frac{S_{n_{2}}}{V_{n_{2}}}\right|^{2}\bigg|D_{1}\right]\leq 2c(2)a^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\Leftrightarrow\mathbb{E}_{P^{2}}\left[\left|\frac{S_{n_{2}}}{V_{n_{2}}}\right|^{2}\bigg|D_{1}\right]\leq\frac{4c(2)a^{2}}{(1-4c(2)C^{\prime}R_{*})_{+}}=:M^{2}.

By Cauchy-Schwarz,
\mathbb{E}_{P^{2}}\left[\left|S_{n_{2}}/{V_{n_{2}}}\right||D_{1}\right]\leq M
, and thus
S_{n_{2}}/V_{n_{2}}
is stochastically bounded. Theorem 2.5 of
Giné et al. (1997)
then gives,
t>0

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{P^{2}}\!\left[\exp\!\left(t\left|\frac{S_{n_{2}}}{V_{n_{2}}}\right|\right)\bigg|D_{1}\right]\leq 2\exp\!\left(2^{-1/2}c(2)M^{2}t^{2}\right).

When
R_{*}\leq C^{\prime\prime}:=1/(4c(2)C^{\prime}R^{*})
, the denominator is bounded away from zero, and thus
M^{2}
is bounded by a universal constant. We conclude that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{P^{1}}\left[\mathbb{P}_{P^{2}}\!\left(\frac{S_{n_{2}}}{V_{n_{2}}}\geq z_{\alpha,n_{2}}\bigg|D_{1}\right)\right]\leq\min\left\{1,C^{\prime}\exp(-z_{\alpha,n_{2}}^{2})\mathbb{P}_{P^{1}}(R_{*}\leq C^{\prime\prime})\right\},

which concludes the result.
∎

Proof of
Theorem

11
.

The result is a direct application of Theorem 1 of
Wang (1996)
to

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{P^{N}}\left(\theta(P^{N})\not\in\widehat{\mathrm{CI}}^{\mathtt{CLT}}_{N,\alpha}|D_{1}\right)=\mathbb{P}_{P^{N}}\left(\sum_{i\in I_{2}}\widehat{\xi}_{i}\geq n_{2}^{1/2}z_{\alpha}\widehat{\sigma}_{\theta(P^{N}),\widehat{\theta}_{1}}+n_{2}\widehat{\mathbb{C}}_{2}|D_{1}\right).

∎

S.5

Proofs from
Section

5

S.5.1

Proof of
Theorem

12

Any element
\theta\in\Theta
in the confidence set
\widehat{\mathrm{CI}}^{\dagger}_{N}
, defined as (
8
), satisfies the following:

\displaystyle\widehat{\M}_{2}(\theta)-\widehat{\M}_{2}(\widehat{\theta}_{1})\leq 0

\displaystyle\quad\Longleftrightarrow\M_{2}(\theta)-\M_{2}(\theta(P^{N}))

\displaystyle\quad\quad\leq-\left((\widehat{\M}_{2}-\M_{2})(\theta)-(\widehat{\M}_{2}-\M_{2})(\theta(P^{N}))\right)+\widehat{\M}_{2}(\widehat{\theta}_{1})-\widehat{\M}_{2}(\theta(P^{N}))

Hence, the confidence set (
8
) is contained as

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathrm{CI}}^{\dagger}_{N}

\displaystyle:=\left\{\theta\in\Theta\,:\,\widehat{\M}_{2}(\theta)-\widehat{\M}_{2}(\widehat{\theta}_{1})\leq 0\right\}

\displaystyle\subseteq\left\{\theta\in\Theta\,:\,\M_{2}(\theta)-\M_{2}(\widehat{\theta}_{1})\right.

\displaystyle\quad\leq\left.-\left((\widehat{\M}_{2}-\M_{2})(\theta)-(\widehat{\M}_{2}-\M_{2})(\theta(P^{N}))\right)+\widehat{\M}_{2}(\widehat{\theta}_{1})-\widehat{\M}_{2}(\theta(P^{N}))\right\}

\displaystyle\subseteq\bigg\{\theta\in\Theta\,:\,c_{0}\|\theta-\theta(P^{N})\|^{1+\gamma}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\left.\left|(\widehat{\M}_{2}-\M_{2})(\theta)-(\widehat{\M}_{2}-\M_{2})(\theta(P^{N}))\right|+|\widehat{\M}_{2}(\widehat{\theta}_{1})-\widehat{\M}_{2}(\theta(P^{N}))|\right\}=:\overline{\mathrm{CI}}^{\dagger}_{N},

where we used
(A1)
. Given
R\geq 0
, we consider the partition of the parameter space
\Theta
into

\displaystyle B:=\left\{\theta\in\Theta\,:\,\|\theta-\theta(P^{N})\|\leq R\right\}\quad\textrm{and}\quad B^{c}:=\left\{\theta\in\Theta\,:\,\|\theta-\theta(P^{N})\|>R\right\}.

In our case we choose

\displaystyle R=2^{M}c_{0}^{-1/(1+\gamma)}\left(r_{n_{1}}^{2/(1+\gamma)}+s_{n_{1},n_{2}}^{1/(1+\gamma)}\right),

(E.6)

where
M
will be specified later.
The goal is to show that the confidence set
\overline{\mathrm{CI}}^{\dagger}_{N}
is contained in
B
with high probability, which implies
\widehat{\mathrm{CI}}^{\dagger}_{N}
is also contained in
B
. It is then equivalent to show that
\overline{\mathrm{CI}}^{\dagger}_{N}
intersects with
B^{c}
with small probability. It then follows that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{*}_{P^{N}}(\overline{\mathrm{CI}}^{\dagger}_{N}\cap B^{c})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\widetilde{P}^{1}}\left[\mathbb{P}^{*}_{P^{2}}(\overline{\mathrm{CI}}^{\dagger}_{N}\cap B^{c})\right]+\beta(r),

where the notation
\widetilde{D}_{1}
comes from coupling arguments as in
Theorem

1
. It remains to evaluate the conditional probability. We have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{*}_{P^{2}}(\overline{\mathrm{CI}}^{\dagger}_{N}\cap B^{c})

\displaystyle\quad\leq\mathbb{P}^{*}_{P^{2}}\left(c_{0}\|\theta-\theta(P^{N})\|^{1+\gamma}\leq 2\left|(\widehat{\M}_{2}-\M_{2})(\theta)-(\widehat{\M}_{2}-\M_{2})(\theta(P^{N}))\right|\cap B^{c}|\widetilde{D}_{1}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\mathbb{P}^{*}_{P^{2}}\left(c_{0}\|\theta-\theta(P^{N})\|^{1+\gamma}\leq 2|\widehat{\M}_{2}(\widehat{\theta}_{1})-\widehat{\M}_{2}(\theta(P^{N}))|\cap B^{c}|\widetilde{D}_{1}\right)=\mathbf{I}+\mathbf{II}.

The second term can be controlled by
(A3)
and the choice (
E.6
). Conditioning on the event where

\displaystyle\Omega_{\mathtt{init}}:=\left\{\mathbb{E}_{P^{2}}[|\widehat{\M}_{2}(\widehat{\theta}_{1})-\widehat{\M}_{2}(\theta(P^{N}))|\widetilde{D}_{1}]\leq C_{\mathtt{init}}s_{n_{1},n_{2}}\right\},

it follows

\displaystyle\mathbf{II}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}^{*}_{P^{2}}\left(c_{0}\|\theta-\theta(P^{N})\|^{1+\gamma}\leq 2|\widehat{\M}_{2}(\widehat{\theta}_{1})-\widehat{\M}_{2}(\theta(P^{N}))|\cap B^{c}|\widetilde{D}_{1}\right)+\mathbb{P}_{\widetilde{P}^{1}}(\Omega^{c}_{\mathtt{init}})

\displaystyle\leq 2\cdot 2^{-M(1+\gamma)}C_{\mathtt{init}}+\varepsilon_{\mathtt{init}}

by Markov inequality. Moving onto the term
\mathbf{I}
, we first observe that

\displaystyle\mathbf{I}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}^{*}_{P^{2}}\left(c_{0}\|\theta-\theta(P^{N})\|^{1+\gamma}\leq 2\left|(\widehat{\M}_{2}-\M_{2})(\theta)-(\widehat{\M}_{2}-\M_{2})(\theta(P^{N}))\right|\right.

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\bigg.\mbox{ for }\|\theta-\theta(P^{N})\|\geq\mathrm{R}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}^{*}_{P^{2}}\left(c_{0}\|\theta-\theta(P^{N})\|^{1+\gamma}\leq 2\left|(\widehat{\M}_{2}-\M_{2})(\theta)-(\widehat{\M}_{2}-\M_{2})(\theta(P^{N}))\right|\right.

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\bigg.\mbox{ for }\|\theta-\theta(P^{N})\|\geq 2^{M}c_{0}^{-1/(1+\gamma)}r_{n_{2}}^{2/(1+\gamma)}\bigg).

We define the “shell”:

S_{j}=\{\theta\in\Theta:2^{j}c_{0}^{-1/(1+\gamma)}r_{n_{2}}^{2/(1+\gamma)}\leq\|\theta-\theta(P)\|<2^{j+1}c_{0}^{-1/(1+\gamma)}r_{n_{2}}^{2/(1+\gamma)}\}

for each
j\in\{0\}\cup\mathbb{N}
. It then follows that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{*}_{P^{2}}\left(c_{0}\|\theta-\theta(P^{N})\|^{1+\gamma}\leq 2\left|(\widehat{\M}_{2}-\M_{2})(\theta)-(\widehat{\M}_{2}-\M_{2})(\theta(P^{N}))\right|\right.

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\bigg.\mbox{ for }\|\theta-\theta(P^{N})\|\geq 2^{M/(1+\gamma)}c_{0}^{-1/(1+\gamma)}r_{n_{2}}^{2/(1+\gamma)}\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad=\mathbb{P}^{*}_{P^{2}}\bigg(\exists\,(j\geq M,\theta\in S_{j})\,:\,c_{0}\|\theta-\theta(P^{N})\|^{1+\gamma}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\leq\left.2\left|(\widehat{\M}_{2}-\M_{2})(\theta)-(\widehat{\M}_{2}-\M_{2})(\theta(P^{N}))\right|\right)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\sum_{j=M}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}^{*}_{P^{2}}\bigg(\exists\,\theta\in S_{j}\,:\,c_{0}\|\theta-\theta(P^{N})\|^{1+\gamma}\leq 2\left|(\widehat{\M}_{2}-\M_{2})(\theta)-(\widehat{\M}_{2}-\M_{2})(\theta(P^{N}))\right|\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\sum_{j=M}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}^{*}_{P^{2}}\bigg(2^{j(1+\gamma)}r_{n_{2}}^{2}\leq 2\sup_{\theta\in S_{j}}\left|(\widehat{\M}_{2}-\M_{2})(\theta)-(\widehat{\M}_{2}-\M_{2})(\theta(P^{N}))\right|\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad\leq 2\sum_{j=M}^{\infty}2^{-j(1+\gamma)}r_{n_{2}}^{-2}\mathbb{E}^{*}_{P^{2}}\bigg[\sup_{\theta\in S_{j}}\left|(\widehat{\M}_{2}-\M_{2})(\theta)-(\widehat{\M}_{2}-\M_{2})(\theta(P^{N}))\right|\bigg]

\displaystyle\quad\leq 2\sum_{j=M}^{\infty}2^{-j(1+\gamma)}r_{n_{2}}^{-2}\phi_{n_{2}}(2^{j+1}c_{0}^{-1/(1+\gamma)}r_{n_{2}}^{2/(1+\gamma)})

\displaystyle\quad\leq 2\sum_{j=M}^{\infty}2^{-j(1+\gamma)}2^{q(j+1)}r_{n_{2}}^{-2}\phi_{n_{2}}(c_{0}^{-1/(1+\gamma)}r_{n_{2}}^{2/(1+\gamma)})

\displaystyle\quad\leq 2\sum_{j=M}^{\infty}2^{-j(1+\gamma)}2^{q(j+1)}.

Now the last term can be written as

\displaystyle 2\sum_{j=M}^{\infty}2^{-j(1+\gamma)}2^{q(j+1)}=2C_{q,\gamma}2^{-M(1+\gamma-q)}\quad\textrm{where}\quad C_{q,\gamma}=\frac{2^{q}}{1-2^{q-(1+\gamma)}}.

where
C_{q,\gamma}
is a constant only depending on
q
and
\gamma
. Putting together we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{*}_{P^{N}}\left(\mathrm{Diam}_{\|\cdot\|}\big(\widehat{\mathrm{CI}}^{\dagger}_{N}\big)>2^{M}c_{0}^{-1/(1+\gamma)}(r_{n_{2}}^{2/(1+\gamma)}+s_{n_{1},n_{2}}^{1/(1+\gamma)})\right)

\displaystyle\quad\leq 2\cdot C_{\mathtt{init}}2^{-M(1+\gamma)}+2\cdot C_{q,\gamma}2^{-M(1+\gamma-q)}+\varepsilon_{\mathtt{init}}+\beta(r)

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim_{q,\gamma,C_{\mathtt{init}}}2^{-M(1+\gamma-q)}+\varepsilon_{\mathtt{init}}+\beta(r).

We conclude the result by choosing
M
to be

\displaystyle M=\frac{\log(\mathfrak{C}/\varepsilon)}{(1+\gamma-q)\cdot\log 2}\quad\textrm{and}\quad 2^{M}=\left(\frac{\mathfrak{C}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{1/(1+\gamma-q)},

where
\mathfrak{C}=2(C_{\mathtt{init}}+C_{q,\gamma})
.

S.5.2

Proof of
Theorem

13

Denoting
m_{\theta}(Z_{i})-m_{\widehat{\theta}_{1}}(Z_{i})=(m_{\theta}-m_{\widehat{\theta}_{1}})(Z_{i})
, we observe that

\displaystyle\widehat{\sigma}^{2}_{\theta,\widehat{\theta}_{1}}

\displaystyle=\frac{n_{2}}{n_{2}-1}\left(\frac{1}{n_{2}}\sum_{i\in I_{2}}\{(m_{\theta}-m_{\widehat{\theta}_{1}})(Z_{i})\}^{2}-\left(\frac{1}{n_{2}}\sum_{j\in I_{2}}\{(m_{\theta}-m_{\widehat{\theta}_{1}})(Z_{i})\right)^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{n_{2}}\sum_{i\in I_{2}}\{(m_{\theta}-m_{\widehat{\theta}_{1}})(Z_{i})\}^{2}.

Hence we have

\displaystyle\widehat{t}_{\alpha}(\theta,\widehat{\theta}_{1})

\displaystyle=z_{\alpha}n_{2}^{-1/2}\sqrt{\frac{2}{n_{2}}\sum_{i\in I_{2}}\{(m_{\theta}-m_{\widehat{\theta}_{1}})(Z_{i})\}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq z_{\alpha}n_{2}^{-1/2}\sqrt{\frac{2}{n_{2}}\sum_{i\in I_{2}}2\{(m_{\theta}-m_{\theta(P^{N})}(Z_{i})\}^{2}+2\{(m_{\theta(P^{N})}-m_{\widehat{\theta}_{1}})(Z_{i})\}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq 2z_{\alpha}n_{2}^{-1/2}\sqrt{\left|\frac{1}{n_{2}}\sum_{i\in I_{2}}\{(m_{\theta}-m_{\theta(P^{N})})(Z_{i})\}^{2}-\frac{1}{n_{2}}\sum_{i\in I_{2}}\E_{P_{i}}[\{(m_{\theta}-m_{\theta(P^{N})})(Z)\}^{2}]\right|}

\displaystyle\quad+2z_{\alpha}n_{2}^{-1/2}\sqrt{\left|\frac{1}{n_{2}}\sum_{i\in I_{2}}\E_{P_{i}}[\{(m_{\theta}-m_{\theta(P^{N})})(Z_{i})\}^{2}]\right|}

\displaystyle\quad+2z_{\alpha}n_{2}^{-1/2}\sqrt{\frac{1}{n_{2}}\sum_{i\in I_{2}}\{(m_{\theta(P^{N})}-m_{\widehat{\theta}_{1}})(Z_{i})\}^{2}}

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{R}_{1}+\mathfrak{R}_{2}+\mathfrak{R}_{3}.

Using this expression, we have

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathrm{CI}}^{\mathtt{CLT}}_{N,\alpha}

\displaystyle:=\left\{\theta\in\Theta\,:\,\widehat{\M}_{2}(\theta)-\widehat{\M}_{2}(\widehat{\theta}_{1})\leq\widehat{t}_{\alpha}(\theta,\widehat{\theta}_{1})\right\}

\displaystyle\subseteq\bigg\{\theta\in\Theta\,:\,c_{0}\|\theta-\theta(P^{N})\|^{1+\gamma}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\left.\left|(\widehat{\M}_{2}-\M_{2})(\theta)-(\widehat{\M}_{2}-\M_{2})(\theta(P^{N}))\right|+|\widehat{\M}_{2}(\widehat{\theta}_{1})-\widehat{\M}_{2}(\theta(P^{N}))|\right.

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\mathfrak{R}_{1}+\mathfrak{R}_{2}+\mathfrak{R}_{3}\bigg\}=:\overline{\mathrm{CI}}^{\mathtt{CLT}}_{N,\alpha}.

We can now use the same logic as the proof of
Theorem

12
. We then have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{*}_{P^{2}|\widetilde{P}^{1}}(\overline{\mathrm{CI}}^{\mathtt{CLT}}_{N,\alpha}\cap B^{c})

\displaystyle\quad\leq\mathbb{P}^{*}_{P^{2}|\widetilde{P}^{1}}\left(c_{0}\|\theta-\theta(P^{N})\|^{1+\gamma}\leq 5\left|(\widehat{\M}_{2}-\M_{2})(\theta)-(\widehat{\M}_{2}-\M_{2})(\theta(P^{N}))\right|\cap B^{c}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\mathbb{P}^{*}_{P^{2}|\widetilde{P}^{1}}\left(c_{0}\|\theta-\theta(P^{N})\|^{1+\gamma}\leq 5|\widehat{\M}_{2}(\widehat{\theta}_{1})-\widehat{\M}_{2}(\theta(P^{N}))|\cap B^{c}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\mathbb{P}^{*}_{P^{2}|\widetilde{P}^{1}}\left(c_{0}\|\theta-\theta(P^{N})\|^{1+\gamma}\leq 5\mathfrak{R}_{1}\cap B^{c}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\mathbb{P}^{*}_{P^{2}|\widetilde{P}^{1}}\left(c_{0}\|\theta-\theta(P^{N})\|^{1+\gamma}\leq 5\mathfrak{R}_{2}\cap B^{c}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\mathbb{P}^{*}_{P^{2}|\widetilde{P}^{1}}\left(c_{0}\|\theta-\theta(P^{N})\|^{1+\gamma}\leq 5\mathfrak{R}_{3}\cap B^{c}\right)=\mathbf{I}+\mathbf{II}+\mathbf{III}+\mathbf{IV}+\mathbf{V}.

The terms
\mathbf{I}
is already controlled in the proof of
Theorem

12
, such that
\mathbf{I}\leq 5\cdot C_{q,\gamma}2^{-M(1+\gamma-q)}
. For
\mathbf{II}
, using Markov inequality after squaring both sides, we get

\displaystyle\mathbf{II}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}^{*}_{P^{2}|\widetilde{P}^{1}}\left(c_{0}\|\theta-\theta(P^{N})\|^{1+\gamma}\leq 5|\widehat{\M}_{2}(\widehat{\theta}_{1})-\widehat{\M}_{2}(\theta(P^{N}))|\cap B^{c}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}^{*}_{P^{2}|\widetilde{P}^{1}}\left(c_{0}^{2}\|\theta-\theta(P^{N})\|^{2+2\gamma}\leq 25|\widehat{\M}_{2}(\widehat{\theta}_{1})-\widehat{\M}_{2}(\theta(P^{N}))|^{2}\cap B^{c}\right).

We observe that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{P^{2}|\widetilde{P}^{1}}[|\widehat{\M}_{2}(\widehat{\theta}_{1})-\widehat{\M}_{2}(\theta(P^{N}))|^{2}]

\displaystyle\quad=\mathbb{E}_{P^{2}|\widetilde{P}^{1}}\left[\left|\frac{1}{n_{2}}\sum_{i\in I_{2}}(m_{\theta(P^{N})}-m_{\widehat{\theta}_{1}})(Z_{i})\right|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\quad=\mathbb{E}_{P^{2}|\widetilde{P}^{1}}\left[\left|\frac{1}{n_{2}}\sum_{i\in I_{2}}\widehat{\xi}_{i}\ \right|^{2}\right]+\left|\frac{1}{n_{2}}\sum_{i\in I_{2}}\mathbb{E}_{P_{i}}[(m_{\theta(P^{N})}-m_{\widehat{\theta}_{1}})(Z_{i})|D_{1}]\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{1}{n_{2}}\mathbb{E}_{P^{2}|\widetilde{P}^{1}}\left[\frac{1}{n_{2}}\sum_{i\in I_{2}}\widehat{\xi}_{i}^{2}\right]+\left|\frac{1}{n_{2}}\sum_{i\in I_{2}}\mathbb{E}_{P_{i}}[(m_{\theta(P^{N})}-m_{\widehat{\theta}_{1}})(Z_{i})|D_{1}]\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{1}{n_{2}}\mathbb{E}_{P^{2}|\widetilde{P}^{1}}\left[\frac{1}{n_{2}}\sum_{i\in I_{2}}\widehat{\xi}_{i}^{2}\right]+\widehat{\mathbb{C}}_{2}^{2}

where we used the fact that
\mathbb{E}_{P^{2}}[\widehat{\xi}_{i}\widehat{\xi}_{j}|D_{1}]=0
due to independence and
\mathbb{E}_{P^{2}}[\widehat{\xi}_{i}|D_{1}]=0
. Hence, conditioning on the event

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Omega}_{\mathtt{init}}:=\left\{\frac{1}{n_{2}}\mathbb{E}_{P^{2}|\widetilde{P}^{1}}\left[\frac{1}{n_{2}}\sum_{i\in I_{2}}\widehat{\xi}_{i}^{2}\right]+\widehat{\mathbb{C}}_{2}^{2}\leq\widetilde{C}_{\mathtt{init}}\widetilde{s}_{n_{1},n_{2}}\right\},

we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{II}\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{*}_{P^{2}|\widetilde{P}^{1}}\left(c_{0}^{2}\|\theta-\theta(P^{N})\|^{2+2\gamma}\leq 25|\widehat{\M}_{2}(\widehat{\theta}_{1})-\widehat{\M}_{2}(\theta(P^{N}))|^{2}\cap B^{c}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 25\cdot\widetilde{C}_{\mathtt{init}}2^{-M(1+\gamma)}+\mathbb{P}(\widetilde{\Omega}_{\mathtt{init}}^{c})\leq 25\cdot\widetilde{C}_{\mathtt{init}}2^{-M(1+\gamma)}+\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{\mathtt{init}}.

For
\mathbf{III}
, we follow the same chain of logic as
\mathbf{I}
in the proof of
Theorem

12
, and

\displaystyle\mathbf{III}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}^{*}_{P^{2}|\widetilde{P}^{1}}\left(c_{0}\|\theta-\theta(P^{N})\|^{1+\gamma}\leq 5\mathfrak{R}_{1}\right.

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\bigg.\mbox{ for }\|\theta-\theta(P^{N})\|\geq 2^{M}c_{0}^{-1/(1+\gamma)}u_{n_{2}}^{2/(1+\gamma)}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}^{*}_{P^{2}|\widetilde{P}^{1}}\left(c_{0}^{2}\|\theta-\theta(P^{N})\|^{2+2\gamma}\leq 25\mathfrak{R}_{1}^{2}\right.

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\bigg.\mbox{ for }\|\theta-\theta(P^{N})\|\geq 2^{M}c_{0}^{-1/(1+\gamma)}u_{n_{2}}^{2/(1+\gamma)}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq 25\cdot 4z_{\alpha}^{2}n_{2}^{-1}\sum_{j=M}^{\infty}2^{-2j(1+\gamma)}u_{n_{2}}^{-4}\omega^{2}_{n_{2}}(2^{j+1}c_{0}^{-1/(1+\gamma)}u_{n_{2}}^{2/(1+\gamma)})

\displaystyle\leq 25\cdot 4z_{\alpha}^{2}n_{2}^{-1}\sum_{j=M}^{\infty}2^{-2j(1+\gamma)}2^{2q(j+1)}u_{n_{2}}^{-4}\omega^{2}_{n_{2}}(c_{0}^{-1/(1+\gamma)}u_{n_{2}}^{2/(1+\gamma)})

\displaystyle\leq 25\cdot 4z_{\alpha}^{2}\sum_{j=M}^{\infty}2^{-2j(1+\gamma)}2^{2q(j+1)}\leq 100\cdot z_{\alpha}^{2}\cdot C_{q,\gamma}^{2}2^{-2M(1+\gamma-q)}.

The bound for
\mathbf{IV}
is analogous. Finally for
\mathbf{V}
, observe that

\displaystyle n_{2}^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{P^{2}|\widetilde{P}^{1}}\left[\frac{1}{n_{2}}\sum_{i\in I_{2}}\{(m_{\theta(P^{N})}-m_{\widehat{\theta}_{1}})(Z_{i})\}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{1}{n_{2}}\mathbb{E}_{P^{2}|\widetilde{P}^{1}}\left[\frac{1}{n_{2}}\sum_{i\in I_{2}}\widehat{\xi}_{i}^{2}\right]+\frac{1}{n_{2}}\mathbb{E}_{P^{2}|\widetilde{P}^{1}}\left[\frac{1}{n_{2}}\sum_{i\in I_{2}}\{\E_{P_{i}}[(m_{\theta(P^{N})}-m_{\widehat{\theta}_{1}})(Z_{i})|D_{1}]\}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{1}{n_{2}}\mathbb{E}_{P^{2}|\widetilde{P}^{1}}\left[\frac{1}{n_{2}}\sum_{i\in I_{2}}\widehat{\xi}_{i}^{2}\right]+\frac{1}{n_{2}}\mathbb{E}_{P^{2}|\widetilde{P}^{1}}\left[\frac{1}{n_{2}}\sum_{i\in I_{2}}\mathbb{C}^{2}_{i}(\widehat{\theta}_{1})\right]\leq\widetilde{s}_{n_{1},n_{2}}^{2}.

Hence, on the event
\widetilde{\Omega}_{\mathtt{init}}
this term is controlled as
\mathbf{II}
. Putting together, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{*}_{P^{N}}\left(\mathrm{Diam}_{\|\cdot\|}\big(\widehat{\mathrm{CI}}^{\mathtt{CLT}}_{N,\alpha}\big)>2^{M}c_{0}^{-1/(1+\gamma)}(r_{n_{2}}^{2/(1+\gamma)}+u_{n_{2}}^{2/(1+\gamma)}+{s^{\prime}}_{n_{1},n_{2}}^{1/(1+\gamma)})\right)

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim C_{q,\gamma}2^{-M(1+\gamma-q)}+\widetilde{C}_{\mathtt{init}}2^{-M(1+\gamma)}+z_{\alpha}^{2}C^{2}_{q,\gamma}2^{-2M(1+\gamma-q)}+\widetilde{C}_{\mathtt{init}}z_{\alpha}^{2}2^{-2M(1+\gamma)}+\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{\mathtt{init}}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\mathfrak{C}(1+2^{-M(1+\gamma-q)}z_{\alpha}^{2})2^{-M(1+\gamma-q)}+\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{\mathtt{init}},

where
\mathfrak{C}
is a constant depending on
q,\gamma,\widetilde{C}_{\mathtt{init}}
. We conclude the claim by choosing
M
to be

\displaystyle M=\frac{\log((1+|z_{\alpha}|)\mathfrak{C}/\varepsilon)}{(1+\gamma-q)\cdot\log 2}\quad\textrm{and}\quad 2^{M}=\left(\frac{\mathfrak{C}(1+|z_{\alpha}|)}{\varepsilon}\right)^{1/(1+\gamma-q)}.

S.5.3

Proof of
Theorem

14

Theorem

14
is a simpler version of the following non-asymptotic bound.

Theorem 29
.

Assume
(A1)
and
(A2)
hold for all
\|\theta-\theta(P^{N})\|\leq\rho
, and that
(A3)
,
(A1-global)
and
(A2-ratio)
hold. Then for
n_{2}\geq N_{2}
where
N_{2}
depends on
\varepsilon_{\mathtt{ratio}}
and
\rho
, and for any
\varepsilon>0

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{*}_{P^{N}}\left(\mathrm{Diam}_{\|\cdot\|}\big(\widehat{\mathrm{CI}}^{\dagger}_{N}\big)\leq C\max\{\mathrm{R}^{\dagger}_{N},\mathrm{Q}^{\dagger}_{N}\mathbf{1}\{\mathrm{Q}^{\dagger}_{N}\geq\rho\}\}\right)\geq 1-\varepsilon^{\circ}-\beta(r),

where
\varepsilon^{\circ}=\varepsilon+\varepsilon_{\mathtt{init}}+\varepsilon_{\mathtt{ratio}}
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{R}^{\dagger}_{N}:=c_{0}^{-1/(1+\gamma)}(r_{n_{2}}^{2/(1+\gamma)}+s_{n_{1},n_{2}}^{1/(1+\gamma)}),\quad\mathrm{Q}^{\dagger}_{N}:=C_{\rho}^{-1}\left(s_{n_{1},n_{2}}\right),

and
C
is a constant depending on
\gamma,q,C_{\mathtt{init}},C_{\mathtt{ratio}},g(\cdot)
and
\varepsilon^{\circ}
.

Proof of
Theorem

29
.

For
\rho>0
, we define the partition of the parameter space as

\displaystyle\Theta_{\rho}:=\{\theta\in\Theta\,:\,\|\theta-\theta(P^{N})\|\leq\rho\}\quad\textrm{and}\quad\Theta_{\rho}^{c}:=\Theta\setminus\Theta_{\rho}.

The proof will proceed by analyzing the two disjoint partition of the confidence set:

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathrm{CI}}^{\dagger}_{N}=(\widehat{\mathrm{CI}}^{\dagger}_{N}\,\cap\,\Theta_{\rho})\,\cup\,(\widehat{\mathrm{CI}}^{\dagger}_{N}\,\cap\,\Theta_{\rho}^{c}).

The first set can be analyzed as the proof of
Theorem

12
assuming
(A1)
and
(A2)
, but their respective requirements holding only on
\Theta_{\rho}
. This result concludes that
\widehat{\mathrm{CI}}^{\dagger}_{N}\,\cap\,\Theta_{\rho}
is contained in the ball with radius
C_{\varepsilon}\mathrm{R}_{N}^{\dagger}
with probability greater than
1-\varepsilon
.

We now turn our attention to
\widehat{\mathrm{CI}}^{\dagger}_{N}\,\cap\,\Theta_{\rho}^{c}
. Any element
\theta\in\Theta_{\rho}^{c}
in the confidence set
\widehat{\mathrm{CI}}_{N}^{\dagger}
satisfies the following:

\displaystyle\widehat{\M}_{2}(\theta)-\widehat{\M}_{2}(\widehat{\theta}_{1})\leq 0

\displaystyle\quad\Longleftrightarrow\M_{2}(\theta)-\M_{2}(\theta(P^{N}))+\left((\widehat{\M}_{2}-\M_{2})(\theta)-(\widehat{\M}_{2}-\M_{2})(\theta(P^{N}))\right)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\leq\widehat{\M}_{2}(\widehat{\theta}_{1})-\widehat{\M}_{2}(\theta(P^{N}))

\displaystyle\quad\Longleftrightarrow\M_{2}(\theta)-\M_{2}(\theta(P^{N}))\left(1+\frac{(\widehat{\M}_{2}-\M_{2})(\theta)-(\widehat{\M}_{2}-\M_{2})(\theta(P^{N}))}{\M_{2}(\theta)-\M_{2}(\theta(P^{N}))}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\leq\widehat{\M}_{2}(\widehat{\theta}_{1})-\widehat{\M}_{2}(\theta(P^{N}))

\displaystyle\quad\Longrightarrow C_{\rho}(\|\theta-\theta(P^{N})\|)\left(1-\sup_{\|\theta-\theta(P^{N})\|>\rho}\left|\frac{(\widehat{\M}_{2}-\M_{2})(\theta)-(\widehat{\M}_{2}-\M_{2})(\theta(P^{N}))}{\M_{2}(\theta)-\M_{2}(\theta(P^{N}))}\right|\right)_{+}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\leq|\widehat{\M}_{2}(\widehat{\theta}_{1})-\widehat{\M}_{2}(\theta(P^{N}))|,

where we used
(A1-global)
in the last step. We define the event

\displaystyle\Omega_{\rho}:=\left\{\sup_{\|\theta-\theta(P^{N})\|>\rho}\left|\frac{(\widehat{\M}_{2}-\M_{2})(\theta)-(\widehat{\M}_{2}-\M_{2})(\theta(P^{N}))}{\M_{2}(\theta)-\M_{2}(\theta(P^{N}))}\right|\leq 1/2\right\}.

Under
(A2-ratio)
for fixed
\varepsilon_{\mathtt{ratio}}>0
, we choose
N_{2}
large enough such that for all
n_{2}\geq N_{2}
,
C_{\mathtt{ratio}}R(n_{2},\rho)\leq 1/2
. Such
N_{2}
exists in view of the limiting nature of
R(n_{2},\rho)
. With such a choice of
N_{2}
, we have
\mathbb{P}_{P^{2}}(\Omega_{\rho}^{c})\leq\varepsilon_{\mathtt{ratio}}
. On the event
\Omega_{\rho}
, the confidence set satisfies the inclusion:

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathrm{CI}}_{N}^{\dagger}\cap\Theta_{\rho}^{c}\subseteq\left\{\theta\in\Theta_{\rho}^{c}\,:\,C_{\rho}(\|\theta-\theta(P^{N})\|)\leq 2|\widehat{\M}_{2}(\widehat{\theta}_{1})-\widehat{\M}_{2}(\theta(P^{N}))|\right\}

with probability greater than
1-\varepsilon_{\mathtt{ratio}}
. Furthermore, by Markov inequality, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{P^{2}}\left(|\widehat{\M}_{2}(\widehat{\theta}_{1})-\widehat{\M}_{2}(\theta(P^{N}))|\geq\frac{\E_{P^{2}}[|\widehat{\M}_{2}(\widehat{\theta}_{1})-\widehat{\M}_{2}(\theta(P^{N}))||D_{1}]}{\varepsilon}\bigg|D_{1}\right)\leq\varepsilon.

Hence,
|\widehat{\M}_{2}(\widehat{\theta}_{1})-\widehat{\M}_{2}(\theta(P^{N}))|\leq\varepsilon^{-1}\E_{P^{2}}[|\widehat{\M}_{2}(\widehat{\theta}_{1})-\widehat{\M}_{2}(\theta(P^{N}))|]
in probability greater than
1-\varepsilon
. Note that this is further bounded by
C_{\mathtt{init}}s_{n_{1},n_{2}}
in view of
(A3)
. By the fact that
\theta(P^{N})
is a unique solution,
C_{\rho}
is an increasing function. Hence,

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathrm{CI}}_{N}^{\dagger}\cap\Theta_{\rho}^{c}\subseteq\left\{\theta\in\Theta_{\rho}^{c}\,:\,\|\theta-\theta(P^{N})\|\leq C_{\rho}^{-1}\big((2/\varepsilon)C_{\mathtt{init}}s_{n_{1},n_{2}}\big)\right\},

(E.7)

with probability greater than
1-\varepsilon-\varepsilon_{\mathtt{init}}
. When the upper bound in (
E.7
) becomes smaller than
\rho
, the right-hand side of the set inclusion becomes an empty set. Hence, we can safely replace the upper bound with

\displaystyle\mathrm{Q}_{N}^{\dagger}\mathbf{1}\{\mathrm{Q}_{N}^{\dagger}\geq\rho\}\quad\text{where}\quad\mathrm{Q}_{N}^{\dagger}=C_{\rho}^{-1}((2/\varepsilon)C_{\mathtt{init}}s_{n_{1},n_{2}}).

Putting together, we have

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathrm{CI}}_{N}^{\dagger}\cap\Theta_{\rho}

\displaystyle\subseteq\left\{\theta\in\Theta\,:\,\|\theta-\theta(P^{N})\|\leq\min(C_{\varepsilon_{1}}\mathrm{R}_{N}^{\dagger},\rho)\right\}\quad\text{with prob. grt. than}\quad 1-\varepsilon_{1}

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathrm{CI}}_{N}^{\dagger}\cap\Theta_{\rho}^{c}

\displaystyle\subseteq\left\{\theta\in\Theta\,:\,\|\theta-\theta(P^{N})\|\leq\mathrm{Q}_{N}^{\dagger}\mathbf{1}\{\mathrm{Q}_{N}^{\dagger}\geq\rho\}\right\}\quad\text{with prob. grt. than}\quad 1-\varepsilon_{2}.

Hence we conclude that

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathrm{CI}}_{N}^{\dagger}

\displaystyle\subseteq\left\{\theta\in\Theta\,:\,\|\theta-\theta(P^{N})\|\leq C_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}\max(\mathrm{R}_{N}^{\dagger},\mathrm{Q}_{N}^{\dagger}\mathbf{1}\{\mathrm{Q}_{N}^{\dagger}\geq\rho\})\right\}

with probability greater than
1-\varepsilon_{1}-\varepsilon_{2}
where the constant
C_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}}
depends on
\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}
.
∎

S.5.4

Proof of
Theorem

15

Theorem

15
is a simpler version of the following non-asymptotic bound.

Theorem 30
.

Assume
Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{N}
are independent,
(A1)
,
(A2)
,
(A4)
hold for all
\|\theta-\theta(P^{N})\|\leq\rho
and that
(A5)
,
(A1-global)
,
(A2-ratio)
, and
(A4-ratio)
hold. Then for
n_{2}\geq N_{2}
where
N_{2}
depends on
\varepsilon_{\mathtt{ratio}},\varepsilon_{\mathtt{emp}}
and
\rho
, and for any
\varepsilon>0

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{*}_{P^{N}}\left(\mathrm{Diam}_{\|\cdot\|}\big(\widehat{\mathrm{CI}}^{\mathtt{CLT}}_{N,\alpha}\big)\leq C\max\{(1+|z_{\alpha}|)^{1/(1+\gamma-q)}\mathrm{R}^{\mathtt{CLT}}_{N},\mathrm{Q}^{\mathtt{CLT}}_{N,\alpha}\mathbf{1}\{\mathrm{Q}^{\mathtt{CLT}}_{N,\alpha}\geq\rho\}\}\right)\geq 1-\varepsilon^{\circ},

where
\varepsilon^{\circ}=\varepsilon+\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{\mathtt{init}}+\varepsilon_{\mathtt{ratio}}+\varepsilon_{\mathtt{emp}}
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{R}_{N}^{\mathtt{CLT}}=c_{0}^{-1/(1+\gamma)}(r_{n_{2}}^{2/(1+\gamma)}+u_{n_{2}}^{2/(1+\gamma)}+\widetilde{s}_{n_{1},n_{2}}^{1/(1+\gamma)}),\quad\mathrm{Q}^{\mathtt{CLT}}_{N,\alpha}:=C_{\rho}^{-1}\left((1+|z_{\alpha}|)\widetilde{s}_{n_{1},n_{2}}\right),

and
C
is a constant depending on
\gamma,q,\widetilde{C}_{\mathtt{init}},C_{\mathtt{ratio}},\widetilde{C}_{\mathtt{emp}},g(\cdot)
and
\varepsilon^{\circ}
.

Proof of
Theorem

30
.

The general proof is analogous to that of
Theorem

29
. In the proof of
Theorem

13
, we have established that

\displaystyle\widehat{t}_{\alpha}(\theta,\widehat{\theta}_{1})

\displaystyle\leq 2z_{\alpha}n_{2}^{-1/2}\sqrt{\left|\frac{1}{n_{2}}\sum_{i\in I_{2}}\{(m_{\theta}-m_{\theta(P^{N})})(Z_{i})\}^{2}-\frac{1}{n_{2}}\sum_{i\in I_{2}}\E_{P_{i}}[\{(m_{\theta}-m_{\theta(P^{N})})(Z)\}^{2}]\right|}

\displaystyle\quad+2z_{\alpha}n_{2}^{-1/2}\sqrt{\left|\frac{1}{n_{2}}\sum_{i\in I_{2}}\E_{P_{i}}[\{(m_{\theta}-m_{\theta(P^{N})})(Z_{i})\}^{2}]\right|}

\displaystyle\quad+2z_{\alpha}n_{2}^{-1/2}\sqrt{\frac{1}{n_{2}}\sum_{i\in I_{2}}\{(m_{\theta(P^{N})}-m_{\widehat{\theta}_{1}})(Z_{i})\}^{2}}.

Any element
\theta\in\Theta_{\rho}^{c}
in the confidence set
\widehat{\mathrm{CI}}_{N,\alpha}^{\mathtt{CLT}}
satisfies the following:

\displaystyle\widehat{\M}_{2}(\theta)-\widehat{\M}_{2}(\widehat{\theta}_{1})\leq\widehat{t}_{\alpha}(\theta,\widehat{\theta}_{1})

\displaystyle\quad\Longrightarrow\left\{\M_{2}(\theta)-\M_{2}(\theta(P^{N}))\right\}\left(1-\mathfrak{R}_{\rho,1}-\mathfrak{R}_{\rho,2}-\mathfrak{R}_{\rho,3}\right)_{+}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\leq|\widehat{\M}_{2}(\widehat{\theta}_{1})-\widehat{\M}_{2}(\theta(P^{N}))|+2z_{\alpha}n_{2}^{-1/2}\sqrt{\frac{1}{n_{2}}\sum_{i\in I_{2}}\{(m_{\theta(P^{N})}-m_{\widehat{\theta}_{1}})(Z_{i})\}^{2}},

where

\displaystyle\mathfrak{R}_{\rho,1}

\displaystyle=\sup_{\|\theta-\theta(P^{N})\|>\rho}\left|\frac{(\widehat{\M}_{2}-\M_{2})(\theta)-(\widehat{\M}_{2}-\M_{2})(\theta(P^{N}))}{\M_{2}(\theta)-\M_{2}(\theta(P^{N}))}\right|

\displaystyle\mathfrak{R}_{\rho,2}

\displaystyle=2\sup_{\|\theta-\theta(P^{N})\|>\rho}\sqrt{\left|\frac{z_{\alpha}^{2}\sum_{i\in I_{2}}(m_{\theta}-m_{\theta(P^{N})})^{2}-\mathbb{E}_{P_{i}}[(m_{\theta}-m_{\theta(P^{N})})^{2}]}{n_{2}^{2}\{\M_{2}(\theta)-\M_{2}(\theta(P^{N}))\}^{2}}\right|},\quad\textrm{and}

\displaystyle\mathfrak{R}_{\rho,3}

\displaystyle=2\sup_{\|\theta-\theta(P^{N})\|>\rho}\sqrt{\frac{z_{\alpha}^{2}\sum_{i\in I_{2}}\mathbb{E}_{P_{i}}[(m_{\theta}-m_{\theta(P^{N})})^{2}(Z_{i})]}{n_{2}^{2}\{\M_{2}(\theta)-\M_{2}(\theta(P^{N}))\}^{2}}}.

We define the events

\displaystyle\Omega_{\rho,1}

\displaystyle:=\left\{\sup_{\|\theta-\theta(P^{N})\|>\rho}\left|\frac{(\widehat{\M}_{2}-\M_{2})(\theta)-(\widehat{\M}_{2}-\M_{2})(\theta(P^{N}))}{\M_{2}(\theta)-\M_{2}(\theta(P^{N}))}\right|\leq 1/6\right\},\quad\textrm{and}

\displaystyle\Omega_{\rho,2}

\displaystyle:=\left\{2\sup_{\|\theta-\theta(P^{N})\|>\rho}\sqrt{\left|\frac{z_{\alpha}^{2}\sum_{i\in I_{2}}(m_{\theta}-m_{\theta(P^{N})})^{2}-\mathbb{E}_{P_{i}}[(m_{\theta}-m_{\theta(P^{N})})^{2}]}{n_{2}^{2}\{\M_{2}(\theta)-\M_{2}(\theta(P^{N}))\}^{2}}\right|}\leq 1/6\right\}.

Under
(A2-ratio)
and
(A4-ratio)
, for fixed
\varepsilon_{\mathtt{ratio}}+\varepsilon_{\mathtt{emp}}>0
, we choose
N_{2}
large enough such that for all
n_{2}\geq N_{2}

\displaystyle C_{\mathtt{ratio}}R(n_{2},\rho)\leq\frac{1}{6},\quad C_{\mathtt{emp}}S_{\mathtt{emp}}(n_{2},\rho,\alpha)\leq\frac{1}{(2\cdot 6)^{2}}\quad\text{and}\quad S_{\mathtt{pop}}(n_{2},\rho,\alpha)\leq\frac{1}{(2\cdot 6)^{2}}.

Such
N_{2}
exists by the fact that
R(n_{2},\rho)
,
S_{\mathtt{emp}}(n_{2},\rho,\alpha)
and
S_{\mathtt{pop}}(n_{2},\rho,\alpha)
all tend to zero. With such choice of
n_{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\left(1-\mathfrak{R}_{\rho,1}-\mathfrak{R}_{\rho,2}-\mathfrak{R}_{\rho,3}\right)_{+}\geq 1/2

with probability greater than
1-\varepsilon_{\mathtt{ratio}}-\varepsilon_{\mathtt{emp}}
. Furthermore, we have established in the proof of
Theorem

13
that under
(A5)
,

\displaystyle|\widehat{\M}_{2}(\widehat{\theta}_{1})-\widehat{\M}_{2}(\theta(P^{N}))|^{2}+2z_{\alpha}^{2}\left(\frac{1}{n_{2}^{2}}\sum_{i\in I_{2}}\{(m_{\theta(P^{N})}-m_{\widehat{\theta}_{1}})(Z_{i})\}^{2}\right)^{2}\lesssim\widetilde{C}_{\mathtt{init}}(1+z_{\alpha}^{2})\widetilde{s}_{n_{1},n_{2}}^{2},

with probability greater than
1-\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{\mathtt{init}}
. The results thus far imply that with probability greater than
1-\varepsilon_{\mathtt{ratio}}-\varepsilon_{\mathtt{emp}}-\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{\mathtt{init}}
,

\displaystyle\left\{2^{-1}\M_{2}(\theta)-\M_{2}(\theta(P^{N}))\right\}^{2}\lesssim C_{\mathtt{init}}(1+z_{\alpha}^{2})\widetilde{s}_{n_{1},n_{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\Rightarrow\left\{2^{-1}\M_{2}(\theta)-\M_{2}(\theta(P^{N}))\right\}\lesssim\sqrt{C_{\mathtt{init}}}(1+|z_{\alpha}|)\widetilde{s}_{n_{1},n_{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\Rightarrow C_{\rho}(\|\theta-\theta(P^{N})\|)\lesssim\sqrt{C_{\mathtt{init}}}(1+|z_{\alpha}|)\widetilde{s}_{n_{1},n_{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\Rightarrow\|\theta-\theta(P^{N})\|\lesssim C^{-1}_{\rho}(\sqrt{C_{\mathtt{init}}}(1+|z_{\alpha}|)\widetilde{s}_{n_{1},n_{2}})

\displaystyle\quad\Rightarrow\|\theta-\theta(P^{N})\|\lesssim g(\sqrt{C_{\mathtt{init}}})C^{-1}_{\rho}((1+|z_{\alpha}|)\widetilde{s}_{n_{1},n_{2}}).

This concludes the claim.
∎

S.5.5

Additional Results on Convergence Rates

A diameter bound is derived for the confidence set based on the lower confidence bound construction (
24
). Validity of this confidence set in its general form was established under no structural assumptions on the optimization problem in
Theorem

6
. The following two additional conditions are needed.

(C1)

There exists a function
\psi_{n_{2}}:\mathbb{R}_{+}\mapsto\mathbb{R}_{+}
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{\eta\in\Theta}\,\E^{*}_{P^{2}}\left[\sup_{\|\theta-\theta(P^{N})\|<\delta}|\widehat{t}_{\alpha}(\theta,\eta)-\widehat{t}_{\alpha}(\theta(P^{N}),\eta)\right]\leq\psi_{n_{2}}(\delta)

(E.8)

for every
n_{2}\geq 1
and
\delta>0
, and
\psi_{n_{2}}(x)/x^{q}
is assumed non-increasing for some
q<1+\gamma
.

(C2)

For every
n_{1},n_{2}\geq 1
and
\varepsilon^{\prime}_{\mathtt{init}}>0
, the initial estimator based on
D_{1}
satisfies

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{P^{1}}\!\left(\mathbb{E}_{P^{2}}[|\widehat{t}_{\alpha}(\theta(P^{N}),\widehat{\theta}_{1})|\,|\,D_{1}]\geq C^{\prime}_{\mathtt{init}}\cdot s^{\prime}_{n_{1},n_{2}}\right)\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}_{\mathtt{init}},

(E.9)

where
s^{\prime}_{n_{1},n_{2}},C^{\prime}_{\mathtt{init}}
are non-negative constants.

Condition
(C1)
is the counterpart of
(A2)
, stated for the modulus for
\widehat{t}_{\alpha}(\theta,\eta)-\widehat{t}_{\alpha}(\theta(P^{N}),\eta)
uniformly over
\eta\in\Theta
. Condition
(C2)
essentially quantifies the rate of convergence of
\widehat{\theta}_{1}
.

Theorem 31
.

Assume
\theta(P^{N})
is the unique solution of (
1
) that satisfies
(A1)
–
(C2)
. Define
u_{n_{2}}
as any value that satisfies

\displaystyle u_{n_{2}}^{-2}\psi_{n_{2}}(c_{0}^{-1/(1+\gamma)}u_{n_{2}}^{2/(1+\gamma)})\leq 1,

(E.10)

and define
r_{n_{2}}
as in (
39
).
Then, for any
n_{1},n_{2}\geq 1
and
\varepsilon>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{*}_{P^{N}}\left(\mathrm{Diam}_{\|\cdot\|}\big(\widehat{\mathrm{CI}}^{\mathtt{LCB}}_{N,\alpha}\big)\leq C\varepsilon^{-1/(1+\gamma-q)}\mathrm{R}_{N}^{\mathtt{LCB}})\right)\geq 1-\varepsilon-\varepsilon_{\mathtt{init}}-\varepsilon^{\prime}_{\mathtt{init}}-\beta(r),

where

\displaystyle\mathrm{R}_{N}^{\mathtt{LCB}}=c_{0}^{-1/(1+\gamma)}(r_{n_{2}}^{2/(1+\gamma)}+u_{n_{2}}^{2/(1+\gamma)}+s_{n_{1},n_{2}}^{1/(1+\gamma)}+{s^{\prime}}_{n_{1},n_{2}}^{1/(1+\gamma)}),

and
C
is a constant depending on
\gamma,q,C_{\mathtt{init}}
, and
C^{\prime}_{\mathtt{init}}
.

The proof is structurally identical to that of
Theorem

12
and we provide a general argument while highlighting the difference from
Theorem

12
. As with
Theorem

12
this result is stated for general stochastic optimization problems and imposes no particular form on
\widehat{t}_{\alpha}(\cdot,\cdot)
, provided
(C1)
and
(C2)
can be verified.

Proof of
Theorem

31
.

The proof is structurally identical to that of
Theorem

12
. As such, we only highlight the differences from
Theorem

12
. First, we define the superset of
\widehat{\mathrm{CI}}^{\mathtt{LCB}}_{N,\alpha}
as follows:

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathrm{CI}}^{\mathtt{LCB}}_{N,\alpha}

\displaystyle:=\left\{\theta\in\Theta\,:\,\widehat{\M}_{2}(\theta)-\widehat{\M}_{2}(\widehat{\theta}_{1})\leq\widehat{t}_{\alpha}(\theta,\widehat{\theta}_{1})\right\}

\displaystyle\subseteq\bigg\{\theta\in\Theta\,:\,c_{0}\|\theta-\theta(P^{N})\|^{1+\gamma}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\left.\left|(\widehat{\M}_{2}-\M_{2})(\theta)-(\widehat{\M}_{2}-\M_{2})(\theta(P^{N}))\right|+|\widehat{\M}_{2}(\widehat{\theta}_{1})-\widehat{\M}_{2}(\theta(P^{N}))|\right.

\displaystyle\quad\quad+|\widehat{t}_{\alpha}(\theta,\widehat{\theta}_{1})-\widehat{t}_{\alpha}(\theta(P^{N}),\widehat{\theta}_{1})|+|\widehat{t}_{\alpha}(\theta(P^{N}),\widehat{\theta}_{1})|\bigg\}=:\overline{\mathrm{CI}}^{\mathtt{LCB}}_{N,\alpha}

We then define
B
and
B^{c}
but now with

\displaystyle R=2^{M}c_{0}^{-1/(1+\gamma)}\left(r_{n_{1}}^{2/(1+\gamma)}+r_{n_{1}}^{2/(1+\gamma)}+s_{n_{1},n_{2}}^{1/(1+\gamma)}+{s^{\prime}}_{n_{1},n_{2}}^{1/(1+\gamma)}\right).

We then have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{*}_{P^{2}|\widetilde{P}^{1}}(\overline{\mathrm{CI}}^{\mathtt{LCB}}_{N,\alpha}\cap B^{c})

\displaystyle\quad\leq\mathbb{P}^{*}_{P^{2}|\widetilde{P}^{1}}\left(c_{0}\|\theta-\theta(P^{N})\|^{1+\gamma}\leq 4\left|(\widehat{\M}_{2}-\M_{2})(\theta)-(\widehat{\M}_{2}-\M_{2})(\theta(P^{N}))\right|\cap B^{c}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\mathbb{P}^{*}_{P^{2}|\widetilde{P}^{1}}\left(c_{0}\|\theta-\theta(P^{N})\|^{1+\gamma}\leq 4|\widehat{\M}_{2}(\widehat{\theta}_{1})-\widehat{\M}_{2}(\theta(P^{N}))|\cap B^{c}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\mathbb{P}^{*}_{P^{2}|\widetilde{P}^{1}}\left(c_{0}\|\theta-\theta(P^{N})\|^{1+\gamma}\leq 4|\widehat{t}_{\alpha}(\theta,\widehat{\theta}_{1})-\widehat{t}_{\alpha}(\theta(P^{N}),\widehat{\theta}_{1})|\cap B^{c}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\mathbb{P}^{*}_{P^{2}|\widetilde{P}^{1}}\left(c_{0}\|\theta-\theta(P^{N})\|^{1+\gamma}\leq 4|\widehat{t}_{\alpha}(\theta(P^{N}),\widehat{\theta}_{1})|\cap B^{c}\right)=\mathbf{I}+\mathbf{II}+\mathbf{III}+\mathbf{IV}.

The first two terms are already controlled in the proof of
Theorem

12
, and we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{I}\leq\frac{4C_{q,\gamma}}{2^{M(1+\gamma-q)}}\quad\textrm{and}\quad\mathbf{II}\leq\frac{4C_{\mathtt{init}}}{2^{M(1+\gamma)}}+\varepsilon_{\mathtt{init}}.

The other two follow analogously. Following the peeling and Markov inequality as in

\displaystyle\mathbf{III}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}^{*}_{P^{2}|\widetilde{P}^{1}}\left(c_{0}\|\theta-\theta(P^{N})\|^{1+\gamma}\leq 4\left|\widehat{t}_{\alpha}(\theta,\widehat{\theta}_{1})-\widehat{t}_{\alpha}(\theta(P^{N}),\widehat{\theta}_{1})\right|\right.

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\bigg.\mbox{ for }\|\theta-\theta(P^{N})\|\geq 2^{M}c_{0}^{-1/(1+\gamma)}u_{n_{2}}^{2/(1+\gamma)}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq 4\sum_{j=M}^{\infty}2^{-j(1+\gamma)}2^{q(j+1)}=\frac{4C_{q,\gamma}}{2^{M(1+\gamma-q)}}.

Similarly, condition on the event where

\displaystyle\Omega^{\prime}_{\mathtt{init}}:=\left\{\mathbb{E}_{P^{2}}[|\widehat{t}_{\alpha}(\theta(P^{N}),\widehat{\theta}_{1})|\widetilde{D}_{1}]\leq C^{\prime}_{\mathtt{init}}s^{\prime}_{n_{1},n_{2}}\right\},

we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbf{IV}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{P^{2}|\widetilde{P}^{1}}\left(c_{0}\|\theta-\theta(P^{N})\|^{1+\gamma}\leq 2|\widehat{t}_{\alpha}(\theta(P^{N}),\widehat{\theta}_{1})|\cap B^{c}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 4\cdot 2^{-M(1+\gamma)}{s^{\prime}}^{-1}_{n_{1},n_{2}}\mathbb{E}_{P^{2}|\widetilde{P}^{1}}[|\widehat{t}_{\alpha}(\theta(P^{N}),\widehat{\theta}_{1})|]+\mathbb{P}_{\widetilde{P}^{1}}({\Omega^{\prime}}_{\mathtt{init}}^{c})\leq\frac{4C^{\prime}_{\mathtt{init}}}{2^{M(1+\gamma)}}+\varepsilon^{\prime}_{\mathtt{init}}

by Markov inequality. Hence, we conclude

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{*}_{P^{N}}\left(\mathrm{Diam}_{\|\cdot\|}\big(\widehat{\mathrm{CI}}^{\mathtt{LCB}}_{N,\alpha}\big)>2^{M}c_{0}^{-1/(1+\gamma)}(r_{n_{2}}^{2/(1+\gamma)}+u_{n_{2}}^{2/(1+\gamma)}+s_{n_{1},n_{2}}^{1/(1+\gamma)}+{s^{\prime}}_{n_{1},n_{2}}^{1/(1+\gamma)})\right)

\displaystyle\quad\leq 4\cdot(C_{\mathtt{init}}+C^{\prime}_{\mathtt{init}})2^{-M(1+\gamma)}+8\cdot C_{q,\gamma}2^{-M(1+\gamma-q)}+\varepsilon_{\mathtt{init}}+\varepsilon^{\prime}_{\mathtt{init}}+\beta(r)

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim_{q,\gamma,C_{\mathtt{init}},C^{\prime}_{\mathtt{init}}}2^{-M(1+\gamma-q)}+\varepsilon_{\mathtt{init}}+\varepsilon^{\prime}_{\mathtt{init}}+\beta(r).

We can choose the same
M
as the proof of
Theorem

12
except now we have
\mathfrak{C}=4(C_{\mathtt{init}}+C^{\prime}_{\mathtt{init}}+2C_{q,\gamma})
.
∎

S.6

Proofs from
Section

7

S.6.1

Proof of
Theorem

16

The key identity, obtained by expanding both squared norms, is

\frac{1}{n_{2}}\sum_{i\in I_{2}}\|Z_{i}-\theta\|_{2}^{2}-\|Z_{i}-\widehat{\theta}_{1}\|_{2}^{2}=\|\widehat{\theta}_{1}-\theta\|_{2}^{2}+2\widehat{H}^{\top}(\widehat{\theta}_{1}-\theta).

(E.11)

Two cases can be proved analogously, and we show the case (2). Write out the set as

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathrm{CI}}_{N,\alpha}^{\mathtt{CLT},2}

\displaystyle=\left\{\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:\,\frac{1}{n_{2}}\sum_{i\in I_{2}}\|Z_{i}-\theta\|_{2}^{2}-\|Z_{i}-\widehat{\theta}_{1}\|_{2}^{2}\leq-\|\widehat{\theta}_{1}-\theta\|_{2}^{2}+n_{2}^{-1/2}z_{\alpha}\widehat{\sigma}_{\widehat{\theta}_{3},\widehat{\theta}_{1}}\right\}

\displaystyle=\left\{\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:\,\|\widehat{\theta}_{1}-\theta\|_{2}^{2}+2\widehat{H}^{\top}(\widehat{\theta}_{1}-\theta)\leq-\|\widehat{\theta}_{1}-\theta\|_{2}^{2}+n_{2}^{-1/2}z_{\alpha}\widehat{\sigma}_{\widehat{\theta}_{3},\widehat{\theta}_{1}}\right\}

\displaystyle=\left\{\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:\,\widehat{H}(\widehat{\theta}_{1}-\theta)+\|\widehat{\theta}_{1}-\theta\|_{2}^{2}\leq n_{2}^{-1/2}z_{\alpha}\widehat{\sigma}_{\widehat{\theta}_{3},\widehat{\theta}_{1}}/2\right\}

\displaystyle=\left\{\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:\,(\widehat{\theta}_{1}-\theta+\widehat{H}/2)^{\top}(\widehat{\theta}_{1}-\theta+\widehat{H}/2)\leq\widehat{H}^{\top}\widehat{H}/4+n_{2}^{-1/2}z_{\alpha}\widehat{\sigma}_{\widehat{\theta}_{3},\widehat{\theta}_{1}}/2\right\},

which is an
\mathbb{R}^{d}
-ball with center given by
\widehat{\theta}_{1}+\widehat{H}/2=(\overline{Z}_{2}+\widehat{\theta}_{1})/2
, and the radius given by
(\|\widehat{H}\|^{2}_{2}/4+n_{2}^{-1/2}z_{\alpha}\widehat{\sigma}_{\widehat{\theta}_{3},\widehat{\theta}_{1}}/2)^{1/2}
.

S.6.2

Proof of
Theorem

17

We begin by writing out

\frac{1}{n_{2}}\sum_{i\in I_{2}}(Y_{i}-\theta^{\top}X_{i})^{2}-(Y_{i}-\widehat{\theta}_{1}^{\top}X_{i})^{2}=(\widehat{\theta}_{1}-\theta)^{\top}\widehat{\Gamma}(\widehat{\theta}_{1}-\theta)+2\widehat{\Lambda}^{\top}(\widehat{\theta}_{1}-\theta),

(E.12)

where we denote

\displaystyle\widehat{\Lambda}={n_{2}}^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_{2}}\widehat{\varepsilon}_{i}X_{i},\quad\textrm{where}\quad\widehat{\varepsilon}_{i}=Y_{i}-\widehat{\theta}_{1}^{\top}X_{i}\quad\textrm{and}\quad\widehat{\Gamma}={n_{2}}^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_{2}}X_{i}X_{i}^{\top}.

Two cases can be proved analogously, and we show the case (2). Write out the set as

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathrm{CI}}_{N,\alpha}^{\mathtt{CLT},2}

\displaystyle=\left\{\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:\,(\widehat{\theta}_{1}-\theta)^{\top}\widehat{\Gamma}(\widehat{\theta}_{1}-\theta)+2\widehat{\Lambda}^{\top}(\widehat{\theta}_{1}-\theta)\leq n_{2}^{-1/2}z_{\alpha}\widehat{\sigma}_{\widehat{\theta}_{3},\widehat{\theta}_{1}}-(\widehat{\theta}_{1}-\theta)^{\top}\widehat{\Gamma}(\widehat{\theta}_{1}-\theta)\right\}

\displaystyle=\left\{\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:\,(\widehat{\theta}_{1}-\theta)^{\top}\widehat{\Gamma}(\widehat{\theta}_{1}-\theta)+\widehat{\Lambda}^{\top}(\widehat{\theta}_{1}-\theta)\leq n_{2}^{-1/2}z_{\alpha}\widehat{\sigma}_{\widehat{\theta}_{3},\widehat{\theta}_{1}}/2\right\}

\displaystyle=\left\{\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:\,(\widehat{\theta}_{1}-\theta+\widehat{\Gamma}^{-1}\widehat{\Lambda}/2)^{\top}\widehat{\Gamma}(\widehat{\theta}_{1}-\theta+\widehat{\Gamma}^{-1}\widehat{\Lambda}/2)\leq\widehat{\Lambda}{\widehat{\Gamma}^{-1}}\widehat{\Lambda}/4+n_{2}^{-1/2}z_{\alpha}\widehat{\sigma}_{\widehat{\theta}_{3},\widehat{\theta}_{1}}/2\right\}

Here, observe that

\displaystyle\widehat{\Gamma}^{-1}\widehat{\Lambda}

\displaystyle=\widehat{\Gamma}^{-1}\left(n_{2}^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_{2}}Y_{i}X_{i}-\widehat{\Gamma}\widehat{\theta}_{1}\right)=\theta_{\mathrm{OLS}}-\widehat{\theta}_{1}\quad\textrm{and}

\displaystyle\widehat{\Lambda}{\widehat{\Gamma}^{-1}}\widehat{\Lambda}

\displaystyle=\widehat{\Lambda}{\widehat{\Gamma}^{-1}}{\widehat{\Gamma}}{\widehat{\Gamma}^{-1}}\widehat{\Lambda}=\|\theta_{\mathrm{OLS}}-\widehat{\theta}_{1}\|_{\widehat{\Gamma}}.

Hence we obtain that

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathrm{CI}}_{N,\alpha}^{\mathtt{CLT},2}=\left\{\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:\,\|(\widehat{\theta}_{1}+\theta_{\mathrm{OLS}})/2-\theta\|_{\widehat{\Gamma}}^{2}\leq\|\theta_{\mathrm{OLS}}-\widehat{\theta}_{1}\|_{\widehat{\Gamma}}^{2}/4+n_{2}^{-1/2}z_{\alpha}\widehat{\sigma}_{\widehat{\theta}_{3},\widehat{\theta}_{1}}/2\right\}.

S.7

Proofs from Statistical Applications

This section contains all proofs associated with the statistical applications.

We frequently use following two lemmas, which becomes useful for the analysis of validity and diameter calculation. Their proofs are postponed to
Section

S.7.6
.

Lemma 32
.

Suppose that there exists a constant
\delta_{0}>0
and a
P_{i}
-dependent mean-zero random vector
G_{i}
, such that

\displaystyle\frac{\E_{P_{i}}[(\widehat{\xi}_{i}-\langle\widehat{\theta}_{1}-\theta(P^{N}),G_{i}\rangle)^{2}|D_{1}]}{\E_{P_{i}}[\langle\widehat{\theta}_{1}-\theta(P^{N}),G_{i}\rangle^{2}|D_{1}]}\leq\varphi(\|\widehat{\theta}_{1}-\theta(P^{N})\|),

for all
i\in I_{2}
and
\|\widehat{\theta}_{1}-\theta(P^{N})\|<\delta_{0}

where
\varphi:\mathbb{R}_{+}\mapsto\mathbb{R}_{+}
is continuous and
\varphi(0)=0
. Then,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{P^{1}}\left[\min\left\{1,C\sum_{i\in I_{2}}\mathbb{E}_{P_{i}}\left[\frac{|\widehat{\xi}_{i}|^{2}}{n_{2}^{2}\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_{2}}\min\left\{1,\,\frac{|\widehat{\xi}_{i}|}{n_{2}\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_{2}^{1/2}}\right\}\bigg|D_{1}\right]\right\}\right]\leq\min\left\{1,C^{\prime}R_{n_{2}}\right\},

where
C,C^{\prime}
are universal constants,

\displaystyle R_{n_{2}}=\inf_{\delta<\delta_{0}}\left\{2\sqrt{\varphi(\delta)}+\mathbb{P}_{P^{1}}(\|\widehat{\theta}_{1}-\theta(P^{N})\|>\delta)\right\}+\sum_{i\in I_{2}}\mathbb{E}_{P_{i}}\left[\frac{|\langle u,G_{i}\rangle|^{2}}{\mathbb{V}_{G}}\min\left\{1,\,\frac{|\langle u,G_{i}\rangle|}{\mathbb{V}_{G}^{1/2}}\right\}\right],

and
\mathbb{V}_{G}=\sum_{i\in I_{2}}\E_{P_{i}}[\langle u,G_{i}\rangle^{2}]
.

The following lemma provides an intermediate bound on
\omega_{n_{2},\mathtt{emp}}

in
(A4)
under moment conditions on the envelope.
The standard empirical process notation is adopted throughout: for a
measurable function
f
,

\displaystyle(\mathbb{P}_{n_{2}}-P^{2})f:=\frac{1}{n_{2}}\sum_{i\in I_{2}}f(Z_{i})-\mathbb{E}_{P^{2}}[f(Z)].

The localized function class and its envelope are defined respectively as

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{\delta}:=\{m_{\theta}-m_{\theta_{0}}:\|\theta-\theta_{0}\|\leq\delta\}\quad\textrm{and}\quad M_{\delta}(z):=\sup_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{\delta}}|m(z)|,

where
M_{\delta}
is assumed measurable.

Lemma 33
.

Let
Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n_{2}}
be independent random variables with law
P^{2}
, let
M_{\delta}

be the envelope defined above, and let
\phi_{n_{2}}(\delta)
be as in
(A2)
.

1.

L^{q}
envelope
: If
\E_{P^{2}}[|M_{\delta}|^{q}]\leq C_{q}
for some
q\geq 2
, then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{*}_{P^{2}}\!\left[\sup_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{\delta}}|(\mathbb{P}_{n_{2}}-P^{2})m^{2}|\right]\leq 16\,n_{2}^{2/q-1}C_{q}^{2/q}+8\cdot 8^{1/q}\,n_{2}^{1/q}C_{q}^{1/q}\,\phi_{n_{2}}(\delta).

2.

Sub-Weibull envelope
: If
\mathbb{E}_{P^{2}}[|M_{\delta}|^{q}]\leq K^{q}q^{q/\gamma}
for all
q\geq 1
, then for
n_{2}>e^{2/\gamma}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{*}_{P^{2}}\!\left[\sup_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{\delta}}|(\mathbb{P}_{n_{2}}-P^{2})m^{2}|\right]

\displaystyle\leq 16n_{2}^{-1}e^{2/\gamma}K^{2}(\gamma\log n_{2})^{2/\gamma}

\displaystyle\quad+16\cdot e^{1/\gamma}K(\gamma\log n_{2})^{1/\gamma}\phi_{n_{2}}(\delta).

S.7.1

High-dimensional Mean Inference

Proof of Theorem
18
.

The proof is an application of
Theorem

3
for
\alpha=1/2
and
Theorem

8
for
\alpha\neq 1/2
. We collect relevant values. First,

\displaystyle\widehat{\xi}_{i}

\displaystyle=\|X_{i}-\widehat{\theta}_{1}\|^{2}-\|X_{i}-\theta(P^{N})\|^{2}-\mathbb{E}_{P_{i}}[\|X_{i}-\widehat{\theta}_{1}\|^{2}-\|X_{i}-\theta(P^{N})\|^{2}|D_{1}].

\displaystyle=2\langle X_{i}-\theta(P^{N}),\theta(P^{N})-\widehat{\theta}_{1}\rangle.

Now, we denote
u\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
such that
\theta(P^{N})-\widehat{\theta}_{1}=\delta_{1}u
. Then

\displaystyle|\widehat{\xi}_{i}|^{2}=4\delta_{1}^{2}\langle X_{i}-\theta(P^{N}),u\rangle^{2}\quad\text{and}\quad\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_{2}=\frac{4\delta_{1}^{2}}{n_{2}^{2}}\sum_{i\in I_{2}}u^{\top}\Sigma_{i}u=\frac{4\delta_{1}^{2}u^{\top}\bar{\Sigma}_{2}u}{n_{2}}.

We then have

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in I_{2}}\mathbb{E}_{P_{i}}\left[\frac{|\widehat{\xi}_{i}|^{2}}{n_{2}^{2}\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_{2}}\min\left\{1,\,\frac{|\widehat{\xi}_{i}|}{n_{2}\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_{2}^{1/2}}\right\}\bigg|D_{1}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\leq\sup_{u\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\sum_{i\in I_{2}}\mathbb{E}_{P_{i}}\left[\frac{\langle X_{i}-\theta(P^{N}),u\rangle^{2}}{n_{2}u^{\top}\bar{\Sigma}_{2}u}\min\left\{1,\,\frac{|\langle X_{i}-\theta(P^{N}),u\rangle|}{\sqrt{n_{2}u^{\top}\bar{\Sigma}_{2}u}}\right\}\right]

\displaystyle\quad=\sup_{u\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\sum_{i\in I_{2}}\mathbb{E}_{P_{i}}\left[\frac{\langle\bar{\Sigma}_{2}^{-1/2}(X_{i}-\theta(P^{N})),u\rangle^{2}}{n_{2}}\min\left\{1,\,\frac{|\langle\bar{\Sigma}_{2}^{-1/2}(X_{i}-\theta(P^{N})),u\rangle|}{\sqrt{n_{2}}}\right\}\right]

\displaystyle\quad=\sup_{u\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\sum_{i\in I_{2}}\mathbb{E}_{P_{i}}\left[\frac{\langle X_{i}^{\circ},u\rangle^{2}}{n_{2}}\min\left\{1,\,\frac{|\langle X_{i}^{\circ},u\rangle|}{\sqrt{n_{2}}}\right\}\right]

The result for
\alpha\neq 1/2
is claimed by applying
Theorem

8
.
